Topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories of symmetric quivers with potential
Tudor Pădurariu and Yukinobu Toda

Abstract
. In previous works, we introduced and studied certain categories called quasi-BPS categories associated to symmetric quivers with potential, preprojective algebras, and local surfaces. They have properties reminiscent of BPS invariants/ cohomologies in enumerative geometry, for example they play important roles in categorical wall-crossing formulas. In this paper, we make the connections between quasi-BPS categories and BPS cohomologies more precise via the cycle map for topological K-theory. We show the existence of filtrations on topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories whose associated graded are isomorphic to the monodromy invariant BPS cohomologies. Along the way, we also compute the topological K-theory of categories of matrix factorizations in terms of the monodromy invariant vanishing cycles (a version of this comparison was already known by work of Blanc-Robalo-Toën-Vezzosi), prove a Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theorem for matrix factorizations, and prove the compatibility between the Koszul equivalence in K-theory and dimensional reduction in cohomology. In a separate paper, we use the results from this paper to show that the quasi-BPS categories of K3 surfaces recover the BPS invariants of the corresponding local surface, which are Euler characteristics of Hilbert schemes of points on K3 surfaces.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Background

The BPS invariants are integer valued invariants, which virtually
count semistable (compactly supported) coherent
sheaves on a smooth complex Calabi-Yau (CY)
3
-fold.
They generalize Donaldson-Thomas (DT) invariants
[
Tho00
]
which are only defined when there are no strictly semistable sheaves,
and they were introduced by Joyce–Song
[
JS12
]
and Kontsevich–Soibelman
[
KS
]

in the study of wall-crossing formulas for DT invariants.
BPS invariants are fundamental enumerative invariants which determine many other enumerative invariants of interest for Calabi-Yau
3
-folds, such as Gromov-Witten, Donaldson-Thomas (DT), or Pandharipande-Thomas invariants,
see the surveys
[
PT14
, Section 2 and a half]
and
[
Tod12
, Section 6]
.

Let
X
be a smooth Calabi-Yau
3
-fold, and consider
v\in H^{\ast}(X,\mathbb{Z})

and a stability condition
\sigma
.
We denote by
M^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
the good moduli space of
\sigma
-semistable (compactly supported) coherent sheaves on
X
of Chern character
v
.
There is an associated BPS invariant, see
[
JS12
, Equation (1.19)]

\displaystyle\Omega^{\sigma}_{X}(v)\in\mathbb{Z}.

It was originally defined as a rational number
in
[
JS12
,
KS
]
, and conjectured to be an integer.
The above integrality conjecture is proved by Davison–Meinhardt
[
DM20
]
for quivers with
potential,
by proving that the BPS invariant is the Euler characteristic of some graded vector space, called
BPS cohomology
.
In the case of a CY 3-fold,
the integrality conjecture can be proved by
combining the result of
[
DM20
]
with
the local description of the moduli stacks
of semistable sheaves via quivers with potential
[
Tod18
]
.
However, natural
BPS cohomology spaces for CY 3-folds are not yet available,
and it is an important problem in enumerative algebraic geometry to define them.
BPS cohomologies may be used to obtain
a version of the wall-crossing formulas
[
JS12
,
KS
]
in
cohomological DT theory
[
BBD+15
]
,
a Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt type theorem for Hall algebras of CY 3-folds which is a global version of the Davison–Meinhardt theorem for Hall algebras of quivers with potential
[
DM20
]
,
and a construction of GV invariants via

sl_{2}
-actions
[
MT18
,
Tod23c
]
.

We note here that BPS cohomology is expected to be defined as the cohomology of a
BPS sheaf
on
M_{X}^{\sigma}(v)
, and that BPS cohomology is
not
part of a general cohomology theory (for topological spaces or algebraic varieties). Indeed, it is expected to be defined only for a restricted class of spaces with singularities analogous to
M^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
(for example, for good moduli spaces of
(-1)
-shifted symplectic stacks
[
PTVV13
]
), which nonetheless provide a new tool to study moduli spaces of sheaves on a CY 3-fold.

Even more, one could attempt to construct a natural dg-category

(1.1)

\mathscr{BPS}^{\sigma}_{X}(v)

which recovers a
2
-periodic version of BPS cohomology (via periodic cyclic homology or topological K-theory
[
Bla16
]
), and thus also the BPS invariant
\Omega^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
.
The BPS cohomology, the BPS category (
1.1
) and the K-theory of (
1.1
) (which we call
BPS K-theory
) are alternatives to their classical counterparts for
M^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
.
One may hope that the constructed BPS spaces are more tractable than their classical counterparts, and that they will have applications in noncommutative algebraic geometry,
such as in the construction of non-commutative crepant resolutions
[
PTe
,
PTd
]
, and in geometric representation theory, for example in the study of quantum groups and their representations
[
Soi16
,
VV
,
RSYZ
,
BR
]
.

Locally, the spaces
M^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
can be described as good moduli spaces of representations of certain Jacobi algebras constructed from a symmetric quiver with potential
[
JS12
,
Tod18
]
.
One could then attempt to construct the BPS category (
1.1
) in two steps: first, construct a BPS category for symmetric quivers with potential; second, glue these categories and obtain (
1.1
).
We refer to
[
HL
,
Tod24
,
Păd23
]
for related arguments
on the second step, i.e. gluing categories locally
defined in terms of quivers with potential.

1.2.
The main result

The purpose of this paper is to complete the first step
mentioned in the previous subsection
for symmetric quivers
Q=(I,E)
with a potential
W
.
For a dimension vector

d=(d^{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
,
we denote by

\mathscr{X}(d)=R(d)/G(d)

the stack of
representations of
Q
of dimension
d
,
and consider its good moduli space

(1.2)

\mathscr{X}(d)\to X(d).

The BPS cohomology of
(Q,W)
is the cohomology of a perverse sheaf (called
BPS sheaf

[
DM20
]
)

\mathcal{BPS}_{d}\in\mathrm{Perv}(X(d)).

In
[
Păda
]
,
the first author
introduced
natural candidates for the category (
1.1
)
called
quasi-BPS categories
in
[
PTa
]
:

(1.3)

\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)\subset\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W).

Here,

\delta
is a (real) character of
G(d)
. The
potential
W
induces a regular function

\mathrm{Tr}\,W\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\to\mathbb{C}

and the category
\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W)
is the
dg-category of matrix factorizations of the function

\mathrm{Tr}\,W
, whose objects
consist of tuples

(1.4)

\displaystyle(\alpha\colon E\rightleftarrows F\colon\beta),\ \alpha\circ\beta=\mathrm{Tr}\,W,\ \beta\circ\alpha=\mathrm{Tr}\,W,

where
E,F
are coherent sheaves on
\mathscr{X}(d)
.
The construction of the subcategory (
1.3
)
is based on the
work by Špenko–Van den Bergh
[
ŠVdB17
]

on non-commutative
resolutions of GIT quotients, and contains matrix factorizations (
1.4
)
such that the weights of
E,F

are contained in a certain polytope of the weight lattice.

For any dg-category, Blanc
[
Bla16
]
defined the topological K-theory spectrum.
In this paper, we study the topological K-theory of the quasi-BPS category

\displaystyle K^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))\in\mathrm{Sp}.

The following is a corollary of the main theorem of this paper, which shows that the topological
K-theory
[
Bla16
]

of the quasi-BPS category (
1.3
)
recovers the dimensions of monodromy
invariant BPS cohomologies:

Theorem 1.1
.

(Corollary
8.17
)

Let

(Q,W)
be a symmetric quiver with potential.
Then, for each dimension vector
d
, there exists a real cocharacter
\delta

such that

(1.5)

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}H^{j}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d})^{\mathrm{inv}}.

Here, the right hand side is the monodromy invariant part of the BPS cohomology.

1.3.
Cohomological DT theory

We briefly review cohomological DT theory, which
was first introduced by Kontsevich–Soibelman
[
KS11
]
,
and later developed by Joyce et al.
[
BBD+15
]
.
Under the assumption
that

M_{X}^{\sigma}(v)
consists of only stable
sheaves, the invariant

\Omega^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
equals
the DT invariant

\mathrm{DT}^{\sigma}_{X}(v)

counting stable sheaves
[
Tho00
]
.
Joyce et al.
[
BBD+15
]
introduced a perverse sheaf
\varphi_{\mathrm{JS}}
on
M^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
whose Euler characteristic recovers the DT invariant:

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{j}\dim H^{j}(M^{\sigma}_{X}(v),\varphi_{\mathrm{JS}})=\mathrm{DT}^{\sigma}_{X}(v).

The cohomology
H^{\ast}(M^{\sigma}_{X}(v),\varphi_{\mathrm{JS}})
has some advantages over
[
Sze16
, Section 7.3]
, and is more computable
[
Sze16
, Section 6.2]
than the singular cohomology of
M^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
.

If there are strictly
\sigma
-semistable sheaves, then
\mathrm{DT}^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
may not be an integer
and is related to the BPS invariant
\Omega_{X}^{\sigma}(v)

via the multiple cover formula
[
JS12
, (1.19)]
.
It is thus more natural to search for categorifications of the (integer valued) BPS invariant
\Omega^{\sigma}_{X}(v)
, namely we look for a BPS sheaf on
M_{X}^{\sigma}(v)

whose hypercohomology (which is the BPS cohomology) recovers
the BPS invariant as its Euler characteristic.
Davison–Meinhardt
[
DM20
]
defined the BPS cohomology for all symmetric quivers with potentials, thus for all local models of moduli spaces of sheaves on CY 3-folds
[
Tod18
]
.
Davison–Hennecart–Schlegel Mejia
[
DHSMb
]
defined it for
X=\mathrm{Tot}_{S}K_{S}=S\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}
, where
S
is a Calabi-Yau surface. For a general CY 3-fold, up to the existence of a certain orientation data, the BPS sheaf for moduli spaces of
one-dimensional sheaves is defined in
[
Tod23c
, Definition 2.11]
.

In the case of

X=\mathrm{Tot}_{S}K_{S}

for a CY surface
S
,
we can also regard, via dimensional reduction, the BPS cohomology in
[
DHSMb
]

as cohomology spaces for good moduli spaces of
semistable sheaves on
S
.
Local models of these moduli spaces are given by moduli of representations of preprojective algebras
[
AS18
,
Davc
]
.
For a quiver
Q^{\circ}
,
the moduli stack
\mathscr{P}(d)
of the representations of its preprojective algebras is a quasi-smooth derived stack
with a good moduli space

\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{cl}}\to P(d).

The BPS sheaf on its tripled quiver with potential
gives rise to the perverse sheaf (called
preprojective BPS sheaf
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d}\in\mathrm{Perv}(P(d))

whose cohomology is the BPS cohomology of the preprojective algebra of
Q^{\circ}
.

1.4.
Categorical DT theory

We are interested in constructing a category (
1.1
) which recovers (and has analogous properties to) the BPS invariants/cohomologies. If there are no strictly
\sigma
-semistable sheaves of Chern character
v
, such a category will recover the DT invariants by taking the Euler characteristic of its periodic cyclic homology, see
[
Tod24
]
for a definition for local surfaces and
[
HHR
]
for a recent work addressing the general case.

In previous works, we introduced and studied quasi-BPS categories in the following cases:

•

for symmetric quivers with potential (
1.3
) in
[
Păda
,
PTe
]
,

•

for preprojective algebras
[
PTa
,
PTe
]
, which we denote by

(1.6)

\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)),

•

for zero-dimensional sheaves on smooth surfaces
[
Păd22
,
PTc
]
, and

•

for semistables sheaves on K3 surfaces
[
PTd
]
.

These categories have analogous properties to BPS cohomologies. Indeed, in the above cases,
there are semiorthogonal decompositions of the categorical Hall algebras
[
Păda
, Theorem 1.1]
,
[
Păd23
,
PTe
,
PTd
]
, or of Donaldson-Thomas categories
[
PTa
,
PTe
]
into products of quasi-BPS categories. These semiorthogonal decompositions are analogous to the PBW theorem for cohomological Hall algebras
[
DM20
,
DHSMb
]
, or of the DT/BPS wall-crossing of
Meinhardt–Reineke for framed quivers
[
MR19
]
.
We also proved categorical versions of the Davison support lemma
[
Davb
, Lemma 4.1]

about the support of BPS sheaves on tripled quivers
[
PTb
,
PTe
,
PTd
]
. However, we observed in
[
PTa
]
that quasi-BPS categories do not categorify BPS cohomology for an arbitrary
\delta
.

1.5.
Matrix factorizations and vanishing cycles

When it is defined, the BPS sheaf
is, locally on
M_{X}^{\sigma}(v)
, isomorphic to the vanishing cycle sheaf
of the IC sheaf of some good moduli space
of a smooth quotient stack, see (
1.11
).
We thus first study vanishing cycle sheaves
for a regular function

f\colon\mathscr{X}\to\mathbb{C},

where
\mathscr{X}=X/G
is a smooth quotient stack with

G
a reductive group and
X
a smooth affine variety
with a
G
-action.

It is well-known that the category of matrix factorizations
\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X},f)
is a categorification of vanishing cohomology
H^{\ast}(\mathscr{X},\varphi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}})
, see
[
Efi18
,
BRTV18
]
.
Let
\mathrm{T}
be the monodromy operator on
\varphi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}}
.
The
monodromy invariant vanishing cycle sheaf

\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}}
is defined to
be the cone of the endomorphism
1-\mathrm{T}
on
\varphi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}}
.
Inspired by
[
Efi18
,
BRTV18
,
BD20
]
,
we construct a Chern character map

(1.7)

\mathrm{ch}\colon K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X},f))\to\prod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}H^{i+2j}(\mathscr{X},\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}}),

which is an isomorphism (after tensoring with
\mathbb{Q}
) if
\mathscr{X}
is a variety, see Subsection
6.2
.
The Chern character map (
1.7
) induces a cycle map on an associated graded of topological K-theory:

(1.8)

\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X},f))\to H^{2\dim\mathscr{X}-i-2\ell}(\mathscr{X},\varphi_{f}^{\mathrm{inv}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}}).

In Section
6
, we discuss functoriality of (
1.7
) and (
1.8
), in particular we prove a Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theorem, see Theorem
6.8
.

1.6.
Quasi-BPS categories for symmetric quivers with potential

We briefly explain the construction of the quasi-BPS categories (
1.3
), and then give a
more precise statement of the main result.
Let
(Q,W)
be a symmetric quiver with potential.
For a dimension vector
d

and a real character
\delta
of
G(d)
, Špenko–Van den Bergh
[
ŠVdB17
]
constructed
a subcategory

(1.9)

\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))

such that the endomorphism algebra of its generator
gives a twisted non-commutative crepant resolution of the
affine variety
X(d)
.
The generator of
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)
is a direct sum of vector bundles
of highest weight contained in
some polytope inside the weight lattice of the maximal torus
T(d)
of
G(d)
, see Subsection
3.7

for details.
The quasi-BPS category

\mathbb{S}(d;\delta):=\mathrm{MF}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta),\mathrm{Tr}\,W)\subset\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W)

is defined
to be the category consisting
of matrix factorizations
(\alpha\colon E\rightleftarrows F\colon\beta)

as in (
1.4
), where
E,F
are direct sums of generating vector bundles of
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)
.

We define a set
S^{d}_{\delta}
of partitions of
d
from the combinatorics of the polytope used to define (
1.9
), see Subsection
8.1
.
For each partition
A\in S^{d}_{\delta}
, there is a corresponding perverse sheaf
\mathcal{BPS}_{A}

on
X(d)
, see Subsection
8.2
. We define

(1.10)

\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}:=\bigoplus_{A\in S^{d}_{\delta}}\mathcal{BPS}_{A}[-\ell(A)],

where
\ell(A)
is the length of the partition
A
.
Its monodromy invariant version
\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}^{\rm{inv}}
is
also defined in Subsection
8.1
.
If
A
is
the length one partition
\{d\}
, then

(1.11)

\mathcal{BPS}_{A}=\mathcal{BPS}_{d}:=\begin{cases}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{X(d)}[-1],\text{ if }X(d)^{\circ}\neq\emptyset,\\
0,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Here
X(d)^{\circ}\subset X(d)
is the open subset
parameterizing simple representations.
There is thus a monodromy action on the cohomology of
\mathcal{BPS}_{d,v}
induced from the monodromy of vanishing cycles.
The following is the main result in this paper:

Theorem 1.2
.

(Theorems
8.14
)

Let
(Q,W)
be a symmetric quiver with potential
and let
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
.
For any
i,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the cycle map (
1.8
) for
(\mathscr{X},f)=(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathop{\rm Tr}W)
induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K_{i}^{\mathrm{top}}\left(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)\right)_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}H^{\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-2\ell-i+1}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}^{\mathrm{inv}}).

We note the following corollary of the above theorem.

Corollary 1.3
.

(Corollary
8.17
)

There is an equality

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}H^{j}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta})^{\rm{inv}}.

For any
d
, there exists
\delta
such that

S_{\delta}^{d}=\{d\}
, see Proposition
8.8
.
Therefore, the above corollary implies Theorem
1.1
.
In the case that
S_{\delta}^{d}

contains non-trivial partitions, the topological K-theory
of quasi-BPS categories may have bigger dimensions
than monodromy invariant BPS cohomologies, and we need
to take into account the direct sum of BPS sheaves as in (
1.10
).

The case of zero potential of Theorem
1.2
is related to the categorification of intersection cohomology for good moduli spaces of smooth symmetric stacks pursued in
[
Pădb
]
.

Theorem 1.4
.

(Theorem
8.18
)

Let
Q=(I,E)
be a symmetric quiver
and let
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
.
If
S_{\delta}^{d}=\{d\}
,
there is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces for all
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
:

\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{IH}^{\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-2\ell-1}(X(d)).

Moreover, we have
K_{1}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}=0
.

1.7.
Main ingredients used in the proof

There are two main ingredients of the proof of Theorem
1.2
.
The first one is
the construction of
a cycle map from the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS category to monodromy invariant
BPS cohomology, see Theorem
8.15
:

(1.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K_{i}^{\mathrm{top}}\left(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)\right)\to H^{\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-2\ell-i+1}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}^{\mathrm{inv}}).

The idea is to construct coproduct-like maps associated with
each partition of
d
on both the topological K-theory of the category of matrix
factorizations
and on the cohomology of monodromy invariant vanishing cycles.
These coproduct-like maps are used to restrict the image of the cycle map of the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS
categories. Indeed,
one can show that the above image lies in the summand
of the monodromy invariant vanishing cycles
corresponding to
S_{\delta}^{d}
under the BBDG decomposition theorem for the pushforward of the vanishing cycle sheaf along the map (
1.2
).

The map (
1.12
) is injective by
construction. Therefore, to show Theorem
1.2
, it is enough to argue that
the dimension of the left hand side in (
1.12
) is bigger than or equal to the
dimension of the right hand side. In order to prove the above inequality of dimensions, we use the second crucial ingredient, which is the theory of relative topological K-theory developed
by Moulinos
[
Mou19
]
.
For a complex variety
M
and
\mathrm{Perf}(M)
-linear dg-category

\mathscr{D}
, it associates a sheaf of spectra

\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})
on
M
. Its rationalization

\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})_{\mathbb{Q}}

is a complex of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces on
M
.
In Subsection
8.5
,
we study
\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
show that it contains

\mathcal{BPS}^{\rm{inv}}_{d,\delta}[\beta^{\pm 1}][-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)]

as a direct summand. Here,
\beta
is a formal variable of degree two.
It implies the desired inequality of the dimensions
mentioned above.

The combination of these two ingredients also implies
the following sheaf theoretic version of Theorem
1.2
, which
is stronger than Corollary
1.3
:

Theorem 1.5
.

(Theorem
8.26
)

There is an isomorphism of (rational) constructible complexes on
X(d)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathcal{BPS}^{\rm{inv}}_{d,\delta}[\beta^{\pm 1}][-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)].

1.8.
Quasi-BPS categories for preprojective algebras

The result of
Theorem
1.2
can be also used, in conjunction with dimensional reduction, to compute the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories for preprojective algebras.
For a quiver
Q^{\circ}
,
consider its tripled quiver with potential
(Q,W)
. The subcategory
(
1.6
) is
Koszul equivalent
[
Isi13
]
to the subcategory of graded matrix factorizations with summands in
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)
:

\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{gr}}(d;\delta):=\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}\left(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta),\mathrm{Tr}\,W\right).

We define perverse sheaves
\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,\delta}
on
P(d)

as a direct sum of preprojective BPS sheaves
as in (
1.10
). There is a cycle map

(1.13)

\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d))\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{2\ell}(\mathscr{P}(d))

induced from the Chern character map of
\mathscr{P}(d)
.

Theorem 1.6
.

(Corollary
10.3
and Theorem
10.6
)

Let
Q^{\circ}
be a quiver,
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{1}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}=0
and the cycle map (
1.13
) induces an isomorphism:

(1.14)

\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}H^{-2\ell}(P(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,\delta}).

As a corollary, we have the following.

Corollary 1.7
.

(Corollary
10.9
)

There is an equality

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}K_{0}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}H^{\rm{even}}(P(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}^{p}).

The moduli stacks of semistable sheaves
on Calabi-Yau surfaces are locally described by
those of representations of preprojective algebras of quivers
[
AS18
,
Davc
]
.
In a separate paper
[
PTd
]
, we use the above
local results
to construct natural categorifications (
1.1
) of the BPS invariants for non-compact Calabi-Yau
3
-folds
X=S\times\mathbb{C}
, where
S
is a K3 surface.

1.9.
Weight-independence

We revisit the discussion from Subsection
1.6
, but the same observations apply in the setting of Subsection
1.8
. Let
Q=(I,E)
be a quiver with an even number of edges between any two different vertices and an odd number of loops at every vertex, and let
W
be a
potential of
Q
. Note that the quivers which locally model moduli of semistable sheaves on a K3 surface are of this form.
For
v\in\mathbb{Z}
, we write
\mathbb{S}(d)_{v}:=\mathbb{S}(d;v\tau_{d})
,
see Subsection
3.1
for the notation.
Note that there are equivalences, where
k\in\mathbb{Z}
:

\mathbb{S}(d)_{v}\simeq\mathbb{S}(d)_{v+k\underline{d}},\,\,\mathbb{S}(d)_{v}\simeq\mathbb{S}(d)^{\text{op}}_{-v}

given by tensoring with the
k
th power of the determinant line bundle and by taking the derived dual, respectively. There are no other obvious relations between
\mathbb{S}(d)_{v}
and
\mathbb{S}(d)_{v^{\prime}}
for
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
. However, by Corollary
1.3
, we obtain:

Corollary 1.8
.

Let
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
be such that
\gcd(v,\underline{d})=\gcd(v^{\prime},\underline{d})
. Let
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then there is an equality of dimensions:

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}(d)_{v^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Note that the statement is reminiscent to the
\chi
-independence phenomenon
[
MS23
,
KK
]
, see especially
[
KK
, Corollary 1.5]
. We observed an analogous statement for quasi-BPS categories of K3 surfaces in
[
PTd
]
. We do not know whether a stronger categorical statement, or at the level of algebraic K-theory, should hold for quivers with potential, see
[
PTd
, Conjecture 1.4]
for a conjecture in the case of K3 surfaces.

It is natural to ask whether one can use a coproduct to define a primitive part

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}

of dimension equal to the dimension of the (total) monodromy invariant BPS cohomology, and thus independent of
v\in\mathbb{Z}
. We defined such spaces in the localized equivariant algebraic K-theory for the tripled quiver with potential in
[
PTb
]
. We do not pursue this idea further in this paper.

1.10.
Plan of the paper

We briefly review the content of each section. In Section
2
, we list the main notation used in the paper, and we mention some constructions and results about categories of constructible sheaves, and about categories of coherent sheaves and matrix factorizations.

In Section
3
, we recall the construction of quasi-BPS categories for symmetric quivers with potential and for preprojective algebras.

In Section
4
, we discuss some results about the topological K-theory of dg-categories (due to Blanc) and of its relative version (due to Moulinos).

In Section
5
, we review the Chern character, the cycle map, and the (topological) Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theorem for quotient stacks. The main tool we use is the approximation of quotient stacks by varieties.

In Section
6
, we revisit the known relation between matrix factorizations and vanishing cohomology, and compute the (relative) topological K-theory of matrix factorizations in terms of (monodromy invariant) vanishing cohomology.

In Section
7
, we compare the Koszul equivalence for dg-categories (and its induced isomorphism in K-theory) with the dimensional reduction theorem in cohomology. In particular, we construct a Chern character map from the topological K-theory of a class of graded matrix factorizations to vanishing cohomology.

Sections
8
and
9
are the main parts of the paper, where we prove the main theorems mentioned in the introduction.

In Section
10
,
we discuss the computations of topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories of preprojective algebras in terms of BPS cohomology. In Section
12
, we discuss an extension of this results to étale covers of moduli of representations of preprojective algebras. These two sections are the main input in computing topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories of local K3 surfaces.

In Section
11
, we discuss some explicit computations of the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories. We mention two examples. First, let
g\geqslant 0
. The coarse space of representations of the
2g+1
loop quiver is the variety of matrix invariants
X(d)=\mathfrak{gl}(d)^{2g+1}/\!\!/G(d)
.
By Theorem
1.4
, we obtain a combinatorial formula for the dimensions of the intersection cohomology
\mathrm{IH}^{\bullet}(X(d))
, which recovers a formula of Reineke
[
Rei12
]
. Second, we compute the topological K-theory for quasi-BPS categories of points in
\mathbb{C}^{3}
, see Proposition
11.11
.

In Section
13
, we prove some technical results used in the previous subsections.

1.11.

T. P. is grateful to Columbia University in New York and to Max Planck Institute for Mathematics in Bonn for their hospitality and financial support during the writing of this paper.
Y. T. is supported by World Premier International Research Center
Initiative (WPI initiative), MEXT, Japan, and JSPS KAKENHI Grant Numbers JP19H01779, JP24H00180.

2.1.
Some notations and conventions

For a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded vector space
V=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}V^{j}
, we set
\widetilde{V}^{i}:=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}V^{i+2j}
.

For a set
S
, let
\#S
be the cardinal of
S
.

For an abelian group
V
, we
write
V_{\mathbb{Q}}:=V\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.

A map of abelian groups
V_{1}\to V_{2}
is
called
an isomorphism (resp. injective) over
\mathbb{Q}

if it is an isomorphism (resp. injective) after tensoring with
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.

We list the main notation used in the paper in
Figure

.

Notation introduced in the paper

2.2.
Stacks and semiorthogonal decompositions

The spaces
\mathscr{X}=X/G
considered are quasi-smooth (derived) quotient stacks over
\mathbb{C}
, where
G
is a reductive group acting on
X
.
Here a morphism of derived stacks
\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{Y}
is
called
quasi-smooth
if its relative cotangent complex
is perfect of amplitude
[-1,1]
, and
\mathscr{X}
is quasi-smooth
if
\mathscr{X}\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}
is quasi-smooth.
The classical truncation of
\mathscr{X}
is denoted by

\mathscr{X}^{\rm{cl}}=X^{\rm{cl}}/G
,
and
l\colon\mathscr{X}^{\rm{cl}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}

is the natural closed immersion.
We assume that
X^{\rm{cl}}
is quasi-projective.
We denote by
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}}
the cotangent complex of

\mathscr{X}
.
When
X
is affine, we denote by
X/\!\!/G
the quotient derived scheme with dg-ring of regular functions
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{G}
.

We use the terminology of
good moduli spaces
of Artin stacks, see
[
Alp13
, Example 8.3]
.
In the case of the quotient stack
\mathscr{X}=X/G
, its good moduli space
is
X/\!\!/G
.

We denote by
D^{b}(\mathscr{X})
the dg-category of bounded derived category of coherent
sheaves on
\mathscr{X}
, which is a pre-triangulated dg-category.
In particular, its homotopy category is a triangulated category.
We will consider semiorthogonal decompositions

(2.1)

D^{b}(\mathscr{X})=\langle\mathcal{C}_{i}\mid i\in I\rangle,

where
I
is a partially ordered set.

Consider a morphism
\pi\colon\mathscr{X}\to S
. We say the semiorthogonal decompositions (
2.1
) is
S
-linear
or

\mathrm{Perf}(S)
-linear
if
\mathcal{C}_{i}\otimes\pi^{*}\mathrm{Perf}(S)\subset\mathcal{C}_{i}
for all
i\in I
.

2.3.
Constructible sheaves

For
\mathscr{X}=X/G
a quotient stack, denote by

D(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))
the derived
category of complexes of
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible sheaves on the classical stack (for the analytic topology)
\mathscr{X}^{\rm{cl}}
and by
D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))
the subcategory of bounded complexes, see
[
Ols07
]
.
For
F\in D(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))
, we use the notation
H^{\bullet}(\mathscr{X},F)
for individual cohomology spaces (that is,
\bullet
is an arbitrary integer) and
H^{\ast}(\mathscr{X},F)
for the total cohomology
H^{\ast}(\mathscr{X},F):=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}H^{i}(\mathscr{X},F)
.
Let
\mathbb{D}
denote the Verdier duality functor on
D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))
, and set
\omega_{\mathscr{X}}:=\mathbb{D}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}^{\rm{cl}}}
. If
\mathscr{X}
is a smooth stack, equidimensional of dimension
d
, then
\omega_{\mathscr{X}}=\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}}[2d]
.

For
\mathscr{X}=X/G
, we denote by

\displaystyle H^{i}(\mathscr{X}):=H^{i}(\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q})=H^{i}_{G}(X,\mathbb{Q}),\ H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{i}(\mathscr{X})=H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{i}(\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q})=H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{i,G}(X,\mathbb{Q})

the singular cohomology of
\mathscr{X}
and
the Borel-Moore homology of
\mathscr{X}
,
respectively (with rational coefficients).
For
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
, there
is an intersection product

(2.2)

\displaystyle H^{i}(\mathscr{X})\otimes H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{j}(\mathscr{X})\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{j-i}(\mathscr{X}).

We denote by
\mathrm{Perv}(\mathscr{X})\subset D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))
the abelian category of perverse sheaves on
\mathscr{X}^{\rm{cl}}
, see
[
LO09
]
.
The truncation functors with respect to the perverse
t-structure is denoted by

\displaystyle{}^{p}\tau^{\leqslant\bullet}\colon D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))\to D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X})),\ {}^{p}\tau^{\geqslant\bullet}\colon D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))\to D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X})),

and the cohomology
functors with respect to the perverse t-structure is denoted by

{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}={}^{p}\tau^{\leqslant\bullet}{}^{p}\tau^{\geqslant\bullet}\colon D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))\to\mathrm{Perv}(\mathscr{X})\in D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X})).

For
F\in D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))
, its total cohomology and perverse cohomology are denoted by:

\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(F):=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{H}^{i}(F)[-i],\ {}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(F):=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{i}(F)[-i].

We say
F\in D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))
is
a shifted perverse sheaf in degree
\ell
if
F[\ell]\in\mathrm{Perv}(\mathscr{X})
and
a shifted perverse sheaf
if there exists
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
F[\ell]\in\mathrm{Perv}(\mathscr{X})
.

2.4.
Nearby and vanishing cycles

For
\mathscr{X}
a smooth quotient stack, let

(2.3)

f\colon\mathscr{X}\to\mathbb{C}

be a regular function. In this paper, we consider regular functions (
2.3
) such that
0\in\mathbb{C}
is the only critical value, equivalently that
\mathrm{Crit}(f)\subset\mathscr{X}_{0}:=f^{-1}(0)
set
theoretically.
We consider the vanishing and nearby cycle functors:

(2.4)

\varphi_{f},\psi_{f}\colon D(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}))\to D(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}_{0})).

We refer to
[
Dav17
, Subsection 2.2]
,
[
DM20
, Proposition 2.13]

for details on vanishing and nearby cycles
for quotient stacks.
By abuse of notation, their push-forward along the closed
immersion
\iota\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}

are also denoted by
\varphi_{f}
,
\psi_{f}
.

From the definition of nearby and vanishing cycle functors,
there is an exact triangle:

\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(-)\to\psi_{f}(-)\to\varphi_{f}(-)\to\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(-)[1].

The functors (
2.4
) restrict to functors

\varphi_{f}[-1],\psi_{f}[-1]\colon\mathrm{Perv}(\mathscr{X})\to\mathrm{Perv}(\mathscr{X}_{0}).

Further,
\varphi_{f}[-1]
and
\psi_{f}[-1]
commute with
the dualizing functor
\mathbb{D}
.
They are also equipped with monodromy operators

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}\colon\psi_{f}(-)\to\psi_{f}(-),\ \mathrm{T}\colon\varphi_{f}(-)\to\varphi_{f}(-).

We will abuse notation and let

\displaystyle\varphi_{f}:=\varphi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}},\ \psi_{f}:=\psi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}},\ \varphi_{f}\mathrm{IC}:=\varphi_{f}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}},\ \psi_{f}\mathrm{IC}:=\psi_{f}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}}.

We may drop
f
from the notation if there is no danger of confusion.

2.5.
Matrix factorizations

We refer to
[
PTa
, Subsection 2.6]
for definitions and references related to categories of matrix factorizations.

Let
\mathscr{X}=X/G
be a quotient stack with
X
affine smooth and
G
reductive. For a regular function
f\colon\mathscr{X}\to\mathbb{C}
, we denote the corresponding dg-category of matrix factorizations by

\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X},f).

Its objects are tuples
\left(\alpha\colon E\rightleftarrows F\colon\beta\right)
such that
E,F\in\mathrm{Coh}(\mathscr{X})
and
\alpha\circ\beta
and
\beta\circ\alpha
are multiplication by
f
.
If
\mathbb{M}\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X})
is a pre-triangulated dg-subcategory, let

\mathrm{MF}(\mathbb{M},f)\subset\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X},f)

be the subcategory consisting of totalizations of tuples
(E,F,\alpha,\beta)
with
E,F\in\mathbb{M}
. The category
\mathbb{M}
has a description in terms of categories of singularities
[
PTa
, Subsection 2.6]
. In this paper, we consider categories
\mathbb{M}
generated by a collection
\mathscr{C}
of vector bundles, then

\mathrm{MF}(\mathbb{M},f)
is the category of matrix factorizations with summands
E,F
which are direct sums of vector bundles in
\mathscr{C}
, see
[
PTa
, Lemma 2.3]
.

Suppose that there exists an extra action of
\mathbb{C}^{*}
on
X
which commutes with the action of
G
on
X
.
Assume that
f
is of weight one with respect to
the above
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-action.
We also consider the dg-category of graded matrix factorizations
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X},f)
.
The objects of
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X},f)
can be described as tuples

(2.5)

\displaystyle(E,F,\alpha\colon E\to F(1),\beta\colon F\to E),

where
E
and
F
are
G\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-equivariant coherent sheaves
on
X
,
(1)
is the twist by the character

G\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\to\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
,
and
\alpha
and
\beta
are
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-equivariant morphisms
such that
\alpha\circ\beta
and
\beta\circ\alpha
are multiplication by
f
.
For a pre-triangulated dg-subcategory
\mathbb{M}\subset D^{b}_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathscr{X})
, we define

\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathbb{M},f)\subset\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X},f)

the subcategory of totalizations of tuples
(E,F,\alpha,\beta)
with
E,F\in\mathbb{M}
.
Alternatively, if
\mathbb{M}
is generated by a collection of
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-equivariant vector bundles
\mathscr{C}
, then

\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathbb{M},f)
is the category of matrix factorizations with summands
E,F
which are direct sums of vector bundles in
\mathscr{C}
.

In this paper, we will consider either ungraded or
graded categories of matrix factorizations.
When considering the tensor product
\mathcal{D}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{D}_{2}
of two categories of matrix factorizations,
we define it in the context of the Thom-Sebastiani theorem:
the tensor product is over
\mathbb{C}(\!(\beta)\!)
for
\beta
of homological degree
-2
in the ungraded case, see
[
Prea
, Theorem 4.1.3]
, and is over
\mathbb{C}
in the graded case, see
[
BFK14
, Corollary 5.18]
.

2.6.
The categories of singularities

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a smooth quotient stack of dimension
d
, and
f\colon\mathscr{X}\to\mathbb{C}
a regular function with
0\in\mathbb{C}
the only singular value. Let
\iota\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}
be the (derived) zero locus of
f
.
The
category of singularities
is defined by

(2.6)

D_{\mathrm{sg}}(\mathscr{X}_{0}):=D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{0})/\text{Perf}(\mathscr{X}_{0}).

It is related to the category of matrix factorizations
as follows:

Theorem 2.1
.

(
[
Orl04
,
EP15
]
)

There is an equivalence

(2.7)

\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X},f)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}D_{\mathrm{sg}}(\mathscr{X}_{0}).

2.7.
The Koszul equivalence

In this subsection, we recall the Koszul equivalences for quasi-smooth
quotient stacks.
Let
\mathscr{X}
be a smooth quotient stack
and let
\eta\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{X}
be a rank
r
vector bundle with a section
s\in\Gamma(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E})
.
Let

(2.8)

j\colon\mathscr{P}:=s^{-1}(0)\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}

be the derived zero locus of
s
, and define
the regular function

(2.9)

f\colon\mathscr{E}^{\vee}\to\mathbb{C}

to be
f(x,v_{x})=\langle s(x),v_{x}\rangle
for
x\in\mathscr{X}
and
v_{x}\in\mathscr{E}^{\vee}|_{x}
.
Let
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\vee}
be the derived zero locus of
f
. Consider
the following diagram

(2.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}^{\vee}|_{\mathscr{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}
acts with weight one on the fibers of
\mathscr{E}^{\vee}
and consider the corresponding graded category of matrix factorizations
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{E}^{\vee},f)
.
The Koszul equivalence is stated
as follows:

Theorem 2.2
.

(
[
Isi13
,
Hir17
,
Tod24
]
)

There is an equivalence

(2.11)

\displaystyle\kappa\colon D^{b}(\mathscr{P})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{E}^{\vee},f).

The functor
\kappa

is given by a tensor product with
the Koszul factorization, see
[
Tod24
, Section 2.3.2]
.
We also have the following explicit description on complexes from the classical stack:

Proposition 2.3
.

The composition

D^{b}(\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{cl}})\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}D^{b}(\mathscr{P})\xrightarrow{\kappa}\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{E}^{\vee},f)

is isomorphic to the functor
j^{\prime}_{*}\eta^{\prime*}l_{*}
.

Proof.

In the case that
s
is a regular section,
the closed immersion
l\colon\mathscr{P}^{\rm{cl}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{P}
is an equivalence
and
\kappa=j_{\ast}^{\prime}\eta^{{}^{\prime}\ast}

coincides with the equivalence constructed in
[
Hir17
]
,
see
[
Tod24
, Remark 2.3.5]
.
Therefore the proposition is obvious.
In general, the proposition follows from
the explicit formula
for
\kappa
via the Koszul factorization
in
[
Tod24
, Section 2.3.2]
.
∎

3.
Review of quasi-BPS categories for symmetric quivers

In this section, we review the definition and properties of
quasi-BPS categories for symmetric quivers.
The references for this section are
[
Păd22
,
Păda
,
PTa
,
PTe
]
.

3.1.
Quivers

Let
Q=(I,E)
be a quiver, where
I
is the set of
vertices and
E
is the set of edges.
For a dimension vector
d=(d^{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
,
we denote by

\mathscr{X}(d)=R(d)/G(d)

the stack of representations of
Q
of dimension
d
, alternatively the stack of representations of dimension
d
of the path algebra
\mathbb{C}[Q]
.
Here
R(d)
,
G(d)
are given by

\displaystyle R(d)=\bigoplus_{(i\to j)\in E}\operatorname{Hom}(V^{i},V^{j}),\ G(d)=\prod_{i\in I}GL(V^{i}),

where
V^{i}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-vector space of dimension
d^{i}
.
Consider the good moduli space

\pi_{d}\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\to X(d):=R(d)/\!\!/G(d).

The quotient variety
X(d)
parametrizes semisimple

Q
-representations. We denote by
X(d)^{\circ}\subset X(d)

the open subset corresponding to simple
Q
-representations.

A
potential
of
W
is an element

W\in\mathbb{C}[Q]/[\mathbb{C}[Q],\mathbb{C}[Q]]
.
It is represented by a linear combination of
cycles in
Q
.
A pair
(Q,W)
is called a
quiver with potential
.
A potential
W
determines a regular function

(3.1)

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Tr}W\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\to\mathbb{C}

given by the trace of the compositions
of maps along with cycles in
W
.

3.2.
Character and cocharacter lattices

Let
Q
be a quiver and
d
a dimension vector.
We denote by
T(d)
a maximal torus of
G(d)
, by
M(d)
the weight lattice of
T(d)
,
and by
\mathfrak{g}(d)
the Lie algebra of
G(d)
.
Let
M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}=M(d)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
. Note that we have

\displaystyle M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}=\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{1\leqslant a\leqslant d^{i}}\mathbb{R}\beta_{a}^{i}

where
\beta_{a}^{i}
for
1\leqslant a\leqslant d^{i}

are the weights of the standard representation of

T(d^{i})
.
We denote by
\mathfrak{S}_{a}
the permutation group on
a
letters (where
a\in\mathbb{N}
),
and by
W_{d}:=\times_{i\in I}\mathfrak{S}_{d^{i}}
the Weyl
group of
G(d)
.

We denote by
M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\subset M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}

the dominant chamber consisting of weights

\displaystyle\chi=\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{a=1}^{d^{i}}c^{i}_{a}\beta^{i}_{a},\ c^{i}_{a}\geqslant c^{i}_{b}\mbox{ for all }i\in I,d^{i}\geqslant a\geqslant b\geqslant 1.

For
\chi\in M(d)^{+}
, we denote by
\Gamma_{G(d)}(\chi)
the irreducible
representation of
G(d)
with highest weight
\chi
.
We denote by
\rho
be half the sum of positive roots of
\mathfrak{g}(d)
,

\displaystyle\rho=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{a>b}(\beta_{a}^{i}-\beta_{b}^{i}).

We denote by
1_{d}
the diagonal cocharacter of
T(d)
, which
acts on
\beta_{a}^{i}
by weight one.
For
d=(d^{i})_{i\in I}
,
denote by
\underline{d}=\sum_{i\in I}d^{i}
. We define the following weights

\displaystyle\sigma_{d}:=\sum_{i\in I,1\leqslant a\leqslant d^{i}}\beta^{i}_{a}\in M(d),\ \tau_{d}:=\frac{\sigma_{d}}{\underline{d}}\in M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}.

Let
N(d)
be the cocharacter lattice of
T(d)
.
We have the perfect pairing

\displaystyle\langle-,-\rangle\colon N(d)\times M(d)\to\mathbb{Z},\ N(d)_{\mathbb{R}}\times M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{R}.

If
\lambda
is a cocharacter of
T(d)
and
V
is a
T(d)
-representation, we may abuse notation and write

\langle\lambda,V\rangle=\langle\lambda,\det(V)\rangle

to simplify the notation.
A cocharacter
\lambda=(\lambda_{a}^{i})\in N(d)

for
i\in I
and
1\leqslant a\leqslant d^{i}
is
antidominant

if
\lambda_{a}^{i}\geqslant\lambda_{b}^{i}
for
1\leqslant b\leqslant a\leqslant d^{i}
.
An antidominant cocharacter of
T(d)
uniquely
determines a decomposition
d=d_{1}+\cdots+d_{k}

in
\mathbb{N}^{I}
,
such that
\lambda
acts on
\beta_{a}^{i}
with weight

\lambda_{j}
for
d_{j-1}^{i}<a\leqslant d_{j}^{i}
.
The partition
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
is called
associated
partition
of
\lambda
.

For a cocharacter
\lambda
of
T(d)
and a

T(d)
-representation
V
, we set

V^{\lambda\geqslant 0}\subset V
to be
spanned by
T(d)
-weights
\beta

of
V
such that
\langle\lambda,\beta\rangle\geqslant 0
.
The subspace
V^{\lambda}\subset V
is defined
to be the
\lambda
-fixed subspace.

3.3.
Framed quivers

For a quiver
Q=(I,E)

and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
,
its
\alpha
-
framed quiver

Q^{\alpha f}=(I^{f},E^{\alpha f})
is defined as follows
[
ER09
]
.
The vertex set is
I^{f}=\{\infty\}\sqcup I

and the edge set is

\displaystyle E^{\alpha f}=E\sqcup\{e_{i,j}\mid i\in I,1\leqslant j\leqslant\alpha\},

where
e_{i,j}
is an edge from
\infty
to
i\in I
.
For
d=(d^{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
,
we set

V^{\alpha}(d)=\bigoplus_{i\in I}(V^{i})^{\oplus\alpha},

where
\dim V^{i}=d^{i}
,
and define

(3.2)

\displaystyle R^{\alpha f}(d):=R(d)\oplus V^{\alpha}(d).

Note that
R^{\alpha f}(d)
is the
the affine space of representations of
Q^{\alpha f}
of dimension
vector
(1,d)
.
The moduli stack of
\alpha
-framed representations of
Q
is given by

\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d):=R^{\alpha f}(d)/G(d).

The character
\sigma_{d}
defines
the GIT stability on
Q^{\alpha f}
,
whose (semi)stable locus
consist of

Q^{\alpha f}
-representations with
no subrepresentations of dimension
(1,d^{\prime})
for
d^{\prime}\neq d
,
see
[
ER09
]
,
[
Tod24
, Lemma 5.1.9]
.
By taking the GIT quotient,
we obtain the smooth quasi-projective variety of
stable framed representations

\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\text{ss}}:=R^{\alpha f}(d)^{\text{ss}}/G(d).

3.4.
The categorical Hall product

For a quiver
Q=(I,E)

and a dimension vector
d
,
let
\lambda
be an antidominant cocharacter of
T(d)
.
We have the attracting and fixed stacks given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}=R(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}/G(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0},\ \mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}=R(d)^{\lambda}/G(d)^{\lambda}=\times_{i=1}^{k}\mathscr{X}(d_{i}).

Here
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
is the
partition associated with
\lambda
.
We have the natural maps

\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{p_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{X(d)^{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{d}}
\textstyle{X(d).}

Here
\pi_{d}
,
\pi_{\lambda}
are good moduli space
morphisms,
and

p_{\lambda}
,
a_{\lambda}
,
q_{\lambda}
are
induced by the inclusions, projection,
respectively

\displaystyle R(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}\hookrightarrow R(d),\ R(d)^{\lambda}\hookrightarrow R(d),\ R(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}\twoheadrightarrow R(d)^{\lambda}.

In this paper, we often use the notation
in the diagram (
3.4
).

The categorical Hall product of
Q

is defined to be the functor
[
Păd22
]
:

(3.7)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda}:=p_{\lambda*}q_{\lambda}^{\ast}\colon\boxtimes_{a=1}^{k}D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d_{a}))\to D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d)).

The above functor is a categorical version
of the cohomological Hall product
[
KS11
]
,
which is induced by
the following sheaf-theoretic one, see
[
DM20
]
:

(3.8)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda}^{\rm{co}}:=p_{\lambda*}q_{\lambda}^{\ast}\colon i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}\to\pi_{d\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}.

Let
W
be a potential of
Q
, and
\mathop{\rm Tr}W

the function on
\mathscr{X}(d)
as in (
3.1
).
The categorical Hall product of
(Q,W)
is defined to
be the functor

(3.9)

\displaystyle p_{\lambda*}q_{\lambda}^{\ast}\colon\boxtimes_{a=1}^{k}\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d_{a}),\mathop{\rm Tr}W)\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathop{\rm Tr}W).

If there is a grading, there is also
a categorical Hall product for graded matrix
factorizations.

3.5.
Doubled quivers and preprojective algebras

Let
Q^{\circ}=(I,E^{\circ})
be a quiver. For an edge
e
of
Q
, denote by
\overline{e}
the edge with opposite orientation.
Let
E^{\circ,d}
be the multiset
E^{\circ,d}=\{e,\overline{e}\mid e\in E\}
.
The
doubled quiver

is defined by

Q^{\circ,d}=(I,E^{\circ,d}).

Let
\mathscr{I}\subset\mathbb{C}[Q^{\circ,d}]
be the two-sided ideal generated by
\sum_{e\in E^{\circ}}[e,\overline{e}]
.
The preprojective algebra of
Q^{\circ}
is
\Pi_{Q^{\circ}}:=\mathbb{C}[Q^{\circ,d}]/\mathscr{I}
.

For
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
, recall the stack of representations of dimension
d
of
Q^{\circ}
:

\mathscr{X}^{\circ}(d)=R^{\circ}(d)/G(d).

The stack of representations of
Q^{\circ,d}
is
given by

\mathscr{Y}(d):=(R^{\circ}(d)\oplus R^{\circ}(d)^{\vee})/G(d).

The stack of representations of the preprojective algebra
\Pi_{Q^{\circ}}
is given by

\mathscr{P}(d):=T^{*}\left(\mathscr{X}^{\circ}(d)\right)=\mu^{-1}(0)/G(d),

where

\mu\colon T^{*}R^{\circ}(d)=R^{\circ}(d)\oplus R^{\circ}(d)^{\vee}\to\mathfrak{g}(d)^{\vee}\cong\mathfrak{g}(d)

is the moment map
defined by the relation

\sum_{e\in E^{\circ}}[e,\overline{e}]
,
and
\mu^{-1}(0)
is the derived zero locus of
\mu
.

The image of
\mu
lies in the traceless Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}(d)_{0}\subset\mathfrak{g}(d)
, and thus induces a map
\mu_{0}\colon T^{*}R^{\circ}(d)\to\mathfrak{g}(d)_{0}
.
The reduced version of the moduli stack
is defined to be

\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}}:=\mu_{0}^{-1}(0)/G(d).

Note that
\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red,cl}}=\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{cl}}
.
We have the good moduli space map:

\pi_{P,d}\colon\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}}\to P(d):=\mu^{-1}(0)^{\rm{cl}}/\!\!/G(d).

3.6.
Tripled quivers with potential

Let
Q^{\circ}=(I,E^{\circ})
be a quiver and let
Q^{\circ,d}=(I,E^{\circ,d})
be
its doubled quiver.

The tripled quiver with potential

(Q,W)

is defined as follows.
The quiver
Q=(I,E)
has set of edges
E=E^{\circ,d}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}\mid i\in I\}
, where
\omega_{i}
is a loop at the vertex
i\in I
. The potential
W
is

W:=\left(\sum_{i\in I}\omega_{i}\right)\left(\sum_{e\in E^{\circ}}[e,\overline{e}]\right)\in\mathbb{C}[Q]/[\mathbb{C}[Q],\mathbb{C}[Q]].

We say
(Q,W)
is a tripled quiver with potential if it is obtained as above for some quiver
Q^{\circ}
.

For a tripled quiver
Q
as above, the moduli stack
of its representations is given by

\displaystyle R(d)=T^{\ast}R^{\circ}(d)\oplus\mathfrak{g}(d)=R^{\circ}(d)\oplus R^{\circ}(d)^{\vee}\oplus\mathfrak{g}(d).

We have the moduli stack of
Q
-representations
together with the regular function determined by
W

\mathop{\rm Tr}W\colon\mathscr{X}(d)=R(d)/G(d)\to\mathbb{C}.

Let
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
acts on the
\mathfrak{g}(d)
-factor on
R(d)

with weight one.
The Koszul equivalence (
2.8
) implies an equivalence

(3.10)

\kappa\colon D^{b}\left(\mathscr{P}(d)\right)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}\left(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W\right).

3.7.
Quasi-BPS categories

Here we recall the definition of quasi-BPS categories for
symmetric quivers with potential.
Here a quiver
Q=(I,E)
is called
symmetric

if for any
i,j\in I
the number of edges from
i
to
j
is the same
as that from
j
to
i
.
Let
(Q,W)
be a symmetric quiver with potential
W
.
For a dimension vector

d=(d^{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
and
w\in\mathbb{Z}
,
let
\mathscr{A}
be the following multiset of
T(d)
-weights on
R(d)
:

\mathscr{A}:=\{\beta^{i}_{a}-\beta^{j}_{b}\mid i,j\in I,(i\to j)\in E,1\leqslant a\leqslant d^{i},1\leqslant b\leqslant d^{j}\}.

The above multiset defines the following polytope in
M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}
:

(3.11)

\mathbf{W}(d):=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sum}_{\beta\in\mathscr{A}}[0,\beta]\subset M(d)_{\mathbb{R}},

where the sums above are Minkowski sums in the space of weights
M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}
.

For a weight
\delta\in M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}^{W_{d}}
,
the subcategory

(3.12)

\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))

is defined to be the full subcategory of
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))
generated by vector bundles
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\otimes\Gamma_{G(d)}(\chi)
, where
\chi\in M(d)^{+}
is
such that

\chi+\rho-\delta\in\mathbf{W}(d).

Note that any complex
F\in D^{b}(B\mathbb{C}^{*})
splits as a direct sum

F=\bigoplus_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{w}

such that
\mathbb{C}^{*}
acts with weight
w
on
F_{w}
. We say
w\in\mathbb{Z}
is a weight of
F
if
F_{w}\neq 0
.
For a cocharacter
\lambda
of
T(d)
, we
define

(3.13)

\displaystyle n_{\lambda}:=\langle\lambda,\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}|_{0}^{\lambda>0}\rangle=\langle\lambda,(R(d)^{\vee})^{\lambda>0}-(\mathfrak{g}(d)^{\vee})^{\lambda>0}\rangle.

The category (
3.12
) has the following alternative description.

Lemma 3.1
.

(
[
PTe
, Corollary 3.11]
)

The category
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)
is the subcategory of
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))
of objects
F\in D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))
such that, for any
\nu\colon B\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathscr{X}(d)

corresponding to a point
x\in R(d)
and a cocharacter

\lambda
of
T(d)
which fixes
x
, the weights of
\nu^{*}F
are contained in the set

\displaystyle\left[-\frac{1}{2}n_{\lambda}+\langle\lambda,\delta\rangle,\frac{1}{2}n_{\lambda}+\langle\lambda,\delta\rangle\right].

The quasi-BPS category for quiver
with potential is defined as follows:

Definition 3.2
.

Let
(Q,W)
be a symmetric quiver with potential.
The
quasi-BPS category
for
(Q,W)
is defined by

(3.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(d;\delta):=\mathrm{MF}\left(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta),\mathrm{Tr}\,W\right)\subset\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathop{\rm Tr}W).

Remark 3.3
.

If there is a grading, the graded quasi-BPS
category

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\rm{gr}}(d;\delta)\subset\mathrm{MF}^{\rm{gr}}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathop{\rm Tr}W)

is defined in a similar way, see Subsection
2.5
for details about categories of graded matrix factorizations.

If
\delta=v\tau_{d}
, we use the notations:

\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}:=\mathbb{M}(d;v\tau_{d}),\ \mathbb{S}(d)_{v}:=\mathbb{S}(d;v\tau_{d}).

3.8.
Quasi-BPS categories for preprojective algebras

Let
Q^{\circ}
be a quiver and
Q^{\circ,d}
its double as in
Subsection
3.5
.
There is a subcategory,
called
preprojective quasi-BPS category
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)),

see also
[
PTe
, Definition 2.14]
for its
definition.
We only use the property that, under the Koszul equivalence (
3.10
),
we have an equivalence :

(3.15)

\kappa\colon\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{gr}}(d;\delta).

Let

\mathscr{X}(d)^{\mathrm{red}}
be defined by

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}(d)^{\mathrm{red}}:=(T^{*}R^{\circ}(d)\oplus\mathfrak{g}(d)_{0})/G(d).

By the Koszul equivalence in Theorem
2.2
, we have
an equivalence

\kappa^{\prime}\colon D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}\big(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\mathrm{red}},\mathrm{Tr}\,W\big).

Similarly to above,
there is a subcategory
called
reduced quasi-BPS category

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}}\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}})

such that, under the Koszul equivalence
\kappa^{\prime}
, we have an equivalence

\kappa^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{gr}}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}}.

Here the right hand side is defined similarly to (
3.14
).

We next discuss the compatibility between reduced and non-reduced quasi-BPS categories.
We have
the decomposition

\mathfrak{g}(d)=\mathfrak{g}(d)_{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}

of
G(d)
-representation, and the projection onto the first factor induces a map
t\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\to\mathscr{X}(d)^{\mathrm{red}}
. Note that
t\circ\mathrm{Tr}\,W=\mathrm{Tr}\,W
. Let
l^{\prime}\colon\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{P}(d)

be the natural closed immersion. The next proposition follows from
[
Tod24
, Lemma 2.4.4]
:

Proposition 3.4
.

The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\mathrm{red}},\mathrm{Tr}\,W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}}

It induces a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{gr}}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{gr}}(d;\delta).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}}

3.9.
Semiorthogonal decompositions

We recall (a simplified version of) the semiorthogonal decompositions
of
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))
, see
[
Păda
, Theorem 1.1]
.
Note that the diagonal cocharacter

1_{d}\colon\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\to G(d)
acts on
R(d)
trivially.
Therefore, there is an orthogonal decomposition

\displaystyle D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))=\bigoplus_{v\in\mathbb{Z}}D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))_{v},

where
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))_{v}
is the subcategory of
weight
v
with respect to the diagonal cocharacter.

Theorem 3.5
.

(
[
Păda
]
)

Let
Q=(I,E)
be a symmetric quiver,
and take a dimension vector
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
.
Let
\delta\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}

such that
\langle 1_{d},\delta\rangle=v\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The category
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)
is right admissible in
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))_{v}
,
so there exist
X(d)
-linear semiorthogonal decompositions:

(3.16)

D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))_{v}=\langle\mathbb{B}(d;\delta),\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)\rangle.

Remark 3.6
.

Applying a version of Theorem
3.5

for the categories of matrix factorizations associated with
tripled quivers with potential and
using the Koszul equivalence, the category
\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)
is right admissible in
D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d))_{v}
, so there is a semiorthogonal decomposition:

(3.17)

D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d))_{v}=\langle\mathbb{A}(d;\delta),\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)\rangle.

Similarly the reduced quasi-BPS category
\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}}
is
right admissible in
D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}})_{v}
.

The following framed version is proved in
[
PTe
]

under some assumption on
Q
and
\delta
, but with explicit description
of the semiorthogonal summands. In this paper, we only use the right admissibility,
which is deduced from Theorem
3.5
.
The proof follows by arguments which already appeared in several
references
[
PTa
,
Tod24
,
KT21
,
PTe
]
, but
the statement is not mentioned so we will include the proof in
Subsection
13.1
.

Proposition 3.7
.

In the same setting of Theorem
3.5
,
there is a
X(d)
-linear semiorthogonal decomposition

\displaystyle D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\rm{ss}})=\langle\mathbb{B}(d;\delta)^{\prime},\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)\rangle.

4.
Topological K-theory of dg-categories

In this section, we review the topological K-theory
of dg-categories due to Blanc
[
Bla16
]

and its relative version due to Moulinos
[
Mou19
]
.
We also prove some lemmas which are not available
in the references.

4.1.
Topological K-theory

We use the notion of
spectrum
, which is an object
representing a generalized cohomology theory and is a central object of study in stable homotopy theory.
Basic references are
[
Ada74
,
May99
]
.
A spectrum consists of a sequence of pointed spaces
E=\{E_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

together with maps
\Sigma E_{n}\to E_{n+1}
where
\Sigma
is the suspension
functor.
There is a notion of homotopy groups of a spectrum
denoted by
\pi_{\bullet}(E)
.
The
\infty
-category of spectra is
constructed in
[
Lura
, Section 1.4]
and
denoted by
\mathrm{Sp}
.
A map of spectra

E\to F

in
\mathrm{Sp}

is called an
equivalence
if it induces
isomorphisms on homotopy groups.

For a topological space
X
, we denote by
K^{\rm{top}}_{i}(X)
its
i
-th
topological K-group.
There is a Chern character map

(4.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon K^{\rm{top}}_{i}(X)\to H^{i+2\ast}(X,\mathbb{Q})

which is an isomorphism over
\mathbb{Q}

if
X
is a finite CW complex
by the Atiyah-Hirzebruch theorem.
The K-groups
K^{\rm{top}}_{\bullet}(X)

are obtained as homotopy groups of a spectrum:
there is a spectrum
KU=\{BU\times\mathbb{Z},U,\ldots\}
,
called
the topological K-theory spectrum
,
such that for
KU_{X}:=[\Sigma^{\infty}X,KU]
, we have that (see
[
May99
, Sections 22-24]
):

\displaystyle K_{\bullet}^{\rm{top}}(X)=\pi_{\bullet}KU_{X}.

Here
[-,KU]
is the mapping spectrum with target
KU

and
\Sigma^{\infty}(-)
is the infinite suspension functor.
We call
K^{\rm{top}}(X)
the
topological K-theory spectrum of
X
.

For a dg-category
\mathscr{D}
,
Blanc
[
Bla16
]

defined the

topological K-theory spectrum
of
\mathscr{D}
, denoted by

(4.2)

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathscr{D})\in\mathrm{Sp}.

For
\mathcal{D}=\mathrm{Perf}(X)
, where
X
is a separated
\mathbb{C}
-scheme of finite type,
we have

K^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{D})\simeq KU_{X}.

For
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we consider its
i
-th rational homotopy group, which is a
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space:

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{D})_{\mathbb{Q}}:=K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{D})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},\ K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{D}):=\pi_{i}(K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathscr{D})).

There is an isomorphism
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{D})\cong K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i+2}(\mathscr{D})
for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
given by multiplication with a Bott element, see
[
Bla16
, Definition 1.6]
.
The topological K-theory spectrum sends exact triangles of dg-categories to exact triangles of spectra
[
Bla16
, Theorem 1.1(c)]
.
We denote the
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded total rational
topological K-theory of
\mathscr{D}
by

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\ast}(\mathscr{D}):=K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{D})\oplus K^{\mathrm{top}}_{1}(\mathscr{D}).

Given a filtration (indexed by integers) on
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{D})
for some
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we consider the associated graded pieces
\mathrm{gr}_{\bullet}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{D})
for
\bullet\in\mathbb{Z}
and we let

\mathrm{gr}_{\ast}K_{i}(\mathscr{D}):=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{gr}_{j}K_{i}(\mathscr{D}).

4.2.
Relative topological K-theory

Here we review the relative version of topological
K-theory developed in
[
Mou19
]
.
Below we use the notation in
[
GS
, Section 2]

for sheaves of spectra.

For a complex variety
M
and an
\infty
-category with arbitrary
small limits, we denote by

\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{C}}(M^{\rm{an}})
the
\infty
-category of

\mathcal{C}
-valued hypersheaves on
M^{\rm{an}}

as in
[
Lurb
, Section 1.3.1]
,
where
M^{\rm{an}}
is the underlying complex analytic
space.
There is a rationalization functor, see
[
GS
, Definition 2.6]

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rat}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(M^{\rm{an}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{D(\mathbb{Q})}(M^{\rm{an}})=D(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(M^{\rm{an}}))

given by
\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{F}\wedge H\mathbb{Q}
.
Here,
H\mathbb{Q}
is the Eilenberg-Maclane spectrum
of
\mathbb{Q}
and
the right hand side is the derived category of sheaves of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector
spaces on
M^{\rm{an}}
.
We will write
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Q}}:=\mathrm{Rat}(F)
.
It satisfies that

\pi_{\bullet}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Q}})=\pi_{\bullet}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.

By
[
GS
, Lemma 2.7]
,
the sheaf of topological K-theory spectra
\underline{KU}_{M}
on
M^{\rm{an}}
satisfies

(4.3)

\displaystyle(\underline{KU}_{M})_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{M}[\beta^{\pm 1}]=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}_{M}[2n],

where
\beta
is of degree
2
.
The above isomorphism follows from the degeneration of
Atiyah-Hirzebruch spectral sequence.
The natural morphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon\underline{KU}_{M}\to(\underline{KU}_{M})_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{M}[\beta^{\pm 1}]

induces the Chern character map (
4.1
) by taking the global section.

Let

\mathrm{Cat}^{\rm{perf}}(M)
be
the
\infty
-category of
\mathrm{Perf}(M)
-linear stable

\infty
-categories.
By
[
Mou19
]
, there is a functor

(4.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}^{\rm{perf}}(M)\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(M^{\rm{an}})

such that, for a Brauer class
\alpha
on
M
,
we have

(4.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathrm{Perf}(M,\alpha))=\underline{KU}^{\hat{\alpha}}_{M}.

In the above,

\mathrm{Perf}(M,\alpha)
is the dg-category of
\alpha
-twisted
perfect complexes,

\hat{\alpha}\in H^{3}(M,\mathbb{Z})
is the class associated with
\alpha

via the natural map
\mathrm{Br}(M)\to H^{3}(M,\mathbb{Z})
,
and the right hand side is the sheaf of
\hat{\alpha}
-twisted topological K-theory spectrum.

For
M=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}
,
(
4.4
)
agrees with (
4.2
), that is

\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})=K^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})
.

4.3.
Topological G-theory

For a complex variety
M
,
we have the embedding
\mathrm{Perf}(M)\subset D^{b}(M)
which
is not equivalence unless
M
is smooth.
A version of topological K-theory for
D^{b}(M)
is
called
topological K-homology
. It is
introduced by Thomason
[
Tho88
]
,
and we denote it by
G^{\rm{top}}_{\bullet}(M)
.
There is a Chern character map

(4.6)

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon G_{i}^{\rm{top}}(M)\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{i+2\ast}(M,\mathbb{Q})

which is an isomorphism over
\mathbb{Q}

if
M
is a complex variety,
see
[
BSG11
, Section 3.6 and Section 6]
or
[
BD82
, Section 11]
.

There is also a spectrum
version
KU_{M,c}^{\vee}
,
called
locally compact supported K-homology
, see
[
HLP20
, Lemma 2.6]
.
In
[
HLP20
, Theorem 2.10]
, it is proved
that there is an equivalence

(4.7)

\displaystyle K^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(M))\simeq KU_{M,c}^{\vee}.

We denote by
\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee}
the sheaf of locally
compact supported K-homology.

If
M
is smooth, then
we have
\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee}\cong\underline{KU}_{M}
. We have the natural map

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee}\to(\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee})_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\omega_{M}[\beta^{\pm 1}]

which induces the Chern character map (
4.6
)
by taking the global section,
where recall that
\omega_{M}=\mathbb{D}\mathbb{Q}_{M}
is the dualizing
complex.
The following is the sheaf version of the equivalence (
4.7
):

Lemma 4.1
.

For a quasi-projective scheme
M
,
there is a natural equivalence

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(M))\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee}.

In particular, we have an equivalence

\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(M))_{\mathbb{Q}}\simeq\omega_{M}[\beta^{\pm 1}]
.

The proof of the above lemma is not
available in references.
We prove it after discussing some
functoriality properties of relative
topological K-theories in the next subsection.

4.4.
Push-forward of relative topological K-theories

We will use the following property of
topological K-theory under proper push-forward.

Theorem 4.2
.

(
[
Mou19
, Proposition 7.8]
,
[
GS
, Theorem 2.12]
)

Let
\phi\colon M\to M^{\prime}
be a proper morphism.
Then, for
\mathscr{D}\in\mathrm{Cat}^{\rm{perf}}(M)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{M^{\prime}}^{\rm{top}}(\phi_{\ast}\mathscr{D})\cong\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D}),

where
\phi_{\ast}\mathscr{D}
is the category
\mathscr{D}

with
\mathrm{Perf}(M^{\prime})
-linear structure induced by

\phi^{\ast}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(M^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Perf}(M)
.

Below we often write
\mathcal{K}_{M^{\prime}}^{\rm{top}}(\phi_{\ast}\mathscr{D})
as

\mathcal{K}_{M^{\prime}}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})
when
\phi
is clear from the context.
An isomorphism as in Theorem
4.2
is not known for
non-proper morphism in general.
The following is a version of Theorem
4.2

for open immersions.
As the proof uses stable motivic homotopy theory as in
[
Mou19
, Proposition 7.7]
and is not self-contained,
we postpone the proof to Subsection
13.3
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
j\colon U\hookrightarrow M
be an open immersion.
Then there is a natural equivalence

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(j_{\ast}\mathrm{Perf}(U))\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U}^{\rm{top}}(\mathrm{Perf}(U)).

We give a proof of Lemma
4.1
using the
above results.

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

Let
i\colon M\hookrightarrow A
be a closed immersion
for a smooth
A

with complement
j\colon U\hookrightarrow A
.
We have an exact sequence of dg-categories

\displaystyle D_{M}^{b}(A)\to D^{b}(A)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}D^{b}(U).

Here
D_{M}^{b}(A)\subset D^{b}(A)
is the subcategory
of objects supported on
M
.
Then there is an exact triangle

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{A}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(M))\to\mathcal{K}_{A}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(A))\to\mathcal{K}_{A}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(U)).

The above exact triangle exists since
\mathcal{K}_{A}^{\rm{top}}(-)
satisfies
devissage, which follows since the algebraic K-theory satisfies the same
property, see
[
HLP20
, Example 2.3]
.
By Theorem
4.2
and Lemma
4.3
,
the above exact triangle is

\displaystyle i_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(M))\to\mathcal{K}_{A}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(A))\to j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(U)).

On the other hand, there is also an exact triangle,
see
[
HLP20
, Lemma 2.6]
:

(4.10)

\displaystyle i_{\ast}\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee}\to\underline{KU}_{A,c}^{\vee}\to\underline{KU}_{U,c}^{\vee}.

We have the commutative diagram (cf.
[
HLP20
, Equation (2)]
):

\textstyle{i_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}_{A}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{j_{\ast}K_{U}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(U))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{i_{\ast}\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{KU}_{A,c}^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{\ast}\underline{KU}_{U,c}^{\vee}.}

The middle vertical arrow is an equivalence
since
A
is smooth, so
D^{b}(A)=\mathrm{Perf}(A)
,

\underline{KU}_{A}\cong\underline{KU}_{A,c}^{\vee}
. Similarly the right vertical arrow is also
an equivalence.
Therefore we obtain an equivalence (
4.8
).
The proof that the above equivalence is an independent of a choice of
M\hookrightarrow A

up to homotopy follows from the same argument of
[
HLP20
, Lemma 2.9]
, and we omit the details.
∎

4.5.
Relative topological K-theories of semiorthogonal summands

We also need a version of Theorem
4.2
for global sections over non-proper
schemes. We prove it for semiorthogonal summands of
\mathrm{Perf}(M)
,
D^{b}(M)
, or
of categories of matrix factorization.
We first discuss a preliminary lemma:

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
B
be a quasi-projective scheme.
Then for
\phi\colon B\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}

and
\mathscr{D}\in\mathrm{Cart}^{\rm{perf}}(B)
, there is a natural
morphism

(4.15)

\displaystyle\eta\colon K^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})\to\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D}).

Proof.

When
B
is smooth, the natural morphism (
4.15
)
is constructed in
[
Mou19
, Subsection 7.4]
,
also see
[
GS
, Proposition 2.13]
.
In general, we take a closed immersion

i\colon B\hookrightarrow A
such that
A
is
smooth. Then using

i_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})=\mathcal{K}_{A}^{\rm{top}}(i_{\ast}\mathscr{D})
by Theorem
4.2
,
the desired morphism (
4.15
) is obtained
by applying that for
A
.
∎

We will use the following:

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\mathscr{D}=\langle\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}\rangle

be a
M
-linear semiorthogonal decomposition.
Then we have

(4.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})=\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{C}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{C}_{2}).

Proof.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
F_{i}
be the composition

\displaystyle F_{i}\colon\mathscr{D}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D},

where the first functor is the projection onto the semiorthogonal
summand and the second functor is the inclusion.
Since
F_{i}
is
\mathrm{Perf}(M)
-linear, the functors
F_{i}
induce
the morphisms
F_{i\ast}
on
\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})
giving
idempotents for a splitting (
4.16
).
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
h\colon M\to B
be a proper morphism and consider the constant map
\phi\colon B\to\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\mathscr{D}
be either
\mathrm{Perf}(M)
,
D^{b}(M)

or
\mathrm{MF}(M,f)
for a non-zero function
f
on
M
,
where we assume that
M
is smooth in the last case.
Let
\mathscr{D}=\langle\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}\rangle
be a

B
-linear semiorthogonal decomposition.
Then the natural maps

\displaystyle\eta_{i}\colon K^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{C}_{i})\to\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{C}_{i})

in Lemma
4.4
are equivalences.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.4
, there is a natural map

(4.17)

\displaystyle K^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})\to\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}^{\rm{top}}_{B}(h_{\ast}\mathscr{D}).

We first show that the above map is an equivalence.
Suppose that
\mathscr{D}=\mathrm{Perf}(M)
.
Then by Theorem
4.2
and
(
4.5
), the map (
4.17
) is identified
with the natural map

\displaystyle KU_{M}\to(\phi\circ h)_{\ast}\underline{KU}_{M}

which is an equivalence as
\underline{KU}_{M}
is a sheaf.

Suppose that
\mathscr{D}=D^{b}(M)
.
By (
4.7
) and Lemma
4.1
the map (
4.17
) is identified with

(4.18)

\displaystyle KU_{M,c}^{\vee}\to(\phi\circ h)_{\ast}\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee}.

Since
\underline{KU}_{M,c}^{\vee}
is a sheaf,
the map (
4.18
) is an equivalence.

Suppose that
\mathscr{D}=\mathrm{MF}(M,f)
.
We set
M_{0}=f^{-1}(0)
.
The category
\mathrm{MF}(M,f)
is equivalent to
the singularity category of
M_{0}
, see
Theorem
2.1
.
Therefore there is an exact sequence of dg-categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perf}(M_{0})\to D^{b}(M_{0})\to\mathscr{D}.

Then we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{K^{\rm{top}}(\mathrm{Perf}(M_{0}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(M_{0}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\rm{top}}(\mathrm{Perf}(M_{0}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(M_{0}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D}),}

where each horizontal sequence is an exact triangle.
By arguments as above, the left and middle vertical maps
are equivalences. Therefore the right vertical map is also an equivalence.

By Lemma
4.5
,
the map (
4.17
) is identified with

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\oplus\eta_{2}\colon\colon K^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{C}_{1})\oplus K^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{C}_{2})\to\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{C}_{1})\oplus\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\rm{top}}(\mathcal{C}_{2}).

Therefore both of
\eta_{1}
,
\eta_{2}
are equivalences.
∎

5.
The Chern character maps of quotient stacks

In this section, we recall the definition of the Chern character maps for quotient stacks. The main tool we use for their construction is the approximation of cohomology theories of quotient stacks by varieties
[
Tot99
,
EG00
]
. We also construct a Chern character map for quasi-smooth quotient stacks and discuss a Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theorem for quasi-smooth morphisms. The results are most probably well known to the experts, but we do not know a reference for them.

5.1.
The Chern character map

Let
\mathscr{X}=X/G
be a quotient stack such that
G
is reductive and
X^{\mathrm{cl}}
is quasi-projective. Let
M\subset G
be a compact Lie group such that
G
is the complexification of
M
.
We denote by

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}):=K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet,M}(X),\ G^{\rm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}):=G^{\rm{top}}_{\bullet,M}(\mathscr{X})

the
M
-equivariant topological K-theory of
X
defined by Atiyah-Segal
[
Seg68
]
,
the
M
-equivariant topological K-homology of
X
defined by Thomason
[
Tho88
]
(which is also referred to as the dual of compactly supported equivariant topological K-theory in the literature), respectively.
We refer to
[
BFM79
]
for a brief review of properties of topological K-theory and K-homology of varieties and of the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theorems for varieties.
For references on topological K-homology, see
[
BD82
]
for the non-equivariant case and
[
HLP20
, Subsection 2.1.2]
for the equivariant case.
By
[
HLP20
, Theorem C and the remark following it]
, we have isomorphisms:

(5.1)

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathscr{X}))\cong K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}),\ K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(D^{b}(\mathscr{X}))\cong G^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}).

Note that
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X})=G^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X})
if
\mathscr{X}
is smooth.

For a quotient stack
\mathscr{X}
, we have Chern character maps for
i\in\mathbb{Z}
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{ch}\colon K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{X})\to\prod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}H^{i+2j}(\mathscr{X}),\ \mathrm{ch}\colon G^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{X})\to\prod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}H_{i+2j}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathscr{X}).

The construction of the above Chern character map will be recalled in
Subsection
5.3
.
As we mentioned in the previous section,
the above Chern character maps are
isomorphisms over
\mathbb{Q}

when
\mathscr{X}
is a complex variety,
or more generally for a scheme of finite
type over
\mathbb{C}
.
However, for a quotient stack
\mathscr{X}
, the
above Chern character maps are not necessarily isomorphisms.
In Section
5.3
, we review the approximation of the Chern character of quotient stacks by Chern characters for varieties following Edidin–Graham
[
EG00
]
.

Remark 5.1
.

The above Chern character maps can be also obtained from the Chern character
maps

\operatorname{ch}\colon K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(-)\to\mathrm{HP}_{i}(-)

from topological K-theory to periodic cyclic homology applied to the dg-categories
\mathrm{Perf}(\mathscr{X})
and
D^{b}(\mathscr{X})
, respectively, see
[
Bla16
, Section 4.4]
.

Remark 5.2
.

Note that both cohomology with coefficients in a constructible sheaf
F
and topological K-theory depend only on the underlying classical stack. Let
l
be the closed immersion

l\colon\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}
.
The pushforward functor induces isomorphisms:

(5.2)

l_{*}\colon H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{cl}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}),\,l_{*}\colon G^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{cl}})\xrightarrow{\sim}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}).

The pullback functor induces isomorphisms:

(5.3)

l^{*}\colon H^{\bullet}(\mathscr{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}),\,l^{*}\colon K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}).

5.2.
Approximation of stacks by varieties

In the study of cohomology theories for quotient stacks, it is useful to
approximate quotient stacks by varieties. We use the method of Totaro
[
Tot99
]
, Edidin–Graham
[
EG00
]
.
