has a corresponding partition with terms
e_{i}
with multiplicity
\sum_{a\geqslant 0}m_{i,a}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant s
.
Below we often regard a partition

\{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k}\}=\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{s}\}
of
d

where
e_{i}
appears
m_{i}
-times
as an element
(e_{i},m_{i,a})
of

\mathscr{P}
such that
m_{i,1}=m_{i}
and
m_{i,a}=0

for
a\geqslant 2
.
We denote by
\mathscr{P}^{\circ}\subset\mathscr{P}

the subset of such tuplets.

We consider the addition maps:

(8.17)

\displaystyle\oplus_{i,a}\colon X(e_{i})^{\times m_{i,a}}\to X(m_{i,a}e_{i}),\ \oplus^{\prime}\colon\times_{i=1}^{s}\times_{a\geqslant 0}X(m_{i,a}e_{i})\to X(d).

Using the above addition maps, we
define the following complexes of
constructible sheaves:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}^{m_{i,a}}\left(\mathrm{IC}_{X(e_{i})}[-2a-1]\right)

\displaystyle:=\oplus_{i,a*}\left(\left(\mathrm{IC}_{X(e_{i})}[-2a-1]\right)^{\boxtimes m_{i,a}}\right)^{\mathfrak{S}_{m_{i,a}}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{A}

\displaystyle:=\oplus^{\prime}_{*}\left(\boxtimes_{1\leqslant i\leqslant s,a\geqslant 0}\mathrm{Sym}^{m_{i,a}}\left(\mathrm{IC}_{X(e_{i})}[-2a-1]\right)\right).

Then
\mathrm{P}_{A}
is supported on the image of
\oplus^{\prime}
and is a shifted perverse sheaf of degree

p_{A}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{a\geqslant 0}m_{i,a}(2a+1),

namely we have that
\mathrm{P}_{A}[p_{A}]\in\mathrm{Perv}(X(d))
.

Let
\alpha
be an even positive natural number.
The following explicit form of the BBDG decomposition theorem for
\pi_{\alpha f,d}
was determined by Meinhardt–Reineke:

Theorem 8.21
.

(
[
MR19
, Proposition 4.3]
)

There is a decomposition:

(8.18)

\pi_{\alpha f,d*}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\text{ss}}}[\dim\mathscr{X}(d)]\right)=\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}}P_{A}.

Remark 8.22
.

The result in loc. cit. is stated as an equality in the Grothendieck group of constructible sheaves, but the above stronger statement holds by the argument in
[
DM20
, Proof of Theorem 4.10]
.

Using Theorem
8.21
,
the following decomposition is obtained by Davison–Meinhardt:

Theorem 8.23
.

(
[
DM20
, Theorem C]
)

There is a decomposition:

(8.19)

\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}=\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{P}}\mathrm{P}_{A}.

In the case that there is a potential
W
, we define

(8.20)

\mathrm{Q}_{A}:=\oplus^{\prime}_{*}\left(\boxtimes_{1\leqslant i\leqslant s,a\geqslant 0}\mathrm{Sym}^{m_{i,a}}\left(\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{X(e_{i})}[-2a-2]\right)\right).

Lemma 8.24
.

We have

\mathrm{Q}_{A}=\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{P}_{A}[-1].

Proof.

The lemma follows from the Thom-Sebastiani theorem.
∎

The proper pushforward commutes with the vanishing cycle functor. The map
\pi_{d}
can be approximated by the proper maps
\pi_{\alpha f,d}
, thus
\pi_{d*}
also commutes with the vanishing cycle functor.
From the above observations and
using
the results of Theorem
8.21
, Theorem
8.23
,
the following vanishing cycle versions of these
are also obtained in
[
DM20
]
:

Theorem 8.25
.

(
[
DM20
]
)

There are decompositions:

(8.21)

\displaystyle\pi_{\alpha f,d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\text{ss}}}[\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-1]\right)=\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}}Q_{A},

(8.22)

\displaystyle\pi_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1]=\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{P}}Q_{A}.

The summands in all the above decompositions are induced via cohomological Hall product.

8.5.
Relative topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories

In this subsection, we prove Theorem
8.14

assuming Theorem
8.15
.
A key point is to
study the relative topological K-theory
of quasi-BPS categorify over
X(d)
, and show that
it contains the BPS sheaf as a direct summand.
Then the dimension of
the K-theory of quasi-BPS category is bigger
than or equal to the dimension of the BPS
cohomology, thus Theorem
8.15
implies
Theorem
8.14
.

Proof of Theorem
8.14
assuming Theorem
8.15
.

By a standard argument (whose proof is postponed to Proposition
9.1
and Lemma
9.6
),
we may assume that
Q
has at least two loops at each vertices
so that
X(d)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
\alpha
be an even positive integer and consider
the proper morphism

\displaystyle\pi_{\alpha,d}\colon\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\rm{ss}}\to\mathscr{X}(d)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}X(d).

For simplicity, we write
\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\rm{ss}}=\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}
and

\pi^{{\dagger}}=\pi_{\alpha,d}
.
The local Chern character map is an isomorphism, see Section
4
:

\operatorname{ch}\colon\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}}(D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}}[\beta^{\pm 1}].

By considering its push-forward to
X(d)
and
using Theorem
4.2
and the isomorphism (
4.3
),
we obtain
the sheaf-theoretic Chern character map

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\pi^{{\dagger}}_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}}(D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\pi^{{\dagger}}_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}}[\beta^{\pm 1}].

Together with Theorem
8.21
, there is an isomorphism

(8.23)

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}}P_{A}[\beta^{\pm 1}][-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)].

By Theorem
3.5
,
the subcategory
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}})

is right admissible and
X(d)
-linear.
Therefore, there is a decomposition by Lemma
4.5
:

(8.24)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}))_{\mathbb{Q}}=\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\oplus K^{\prime}.

In particular,

\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}

is a direct summand of
the right hand side of (
8.23
),
therefore it
splits into the direct sum of its perverse cohomologies.
As it is also
2
-periodic, there exists a subset
\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}^{\prime}\subset\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}

such that the isomorphism (
8.23
) restricts
to the isomorphism

(8.25)

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}^{\prime}}P_{A}[\beta^{\pm 1}][-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)].

For a partition
A\in\mathcal{S}_{\delta}^{d}
, we regard
it as an element of
\mathscr{P}^{\circ}\subset\mathscr{P}
,
see Subsection
8.4
.
Below we show that
\mathcal{S}_{\delta}^{d}\subset\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
We first show the case that
A

is the 1-length partition
A=\{d\}
.
Recall that the open subset

X(d)^{\circ}\subset X(d)
of simple
representations is non-empty.
The maps

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}(d)^{\circ}:=\pi_{d}^{-1}(X(d)^{\circ})\to X(d)^{\circ},\ \mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}\circ}:=(\pi^{{\dagger}})^{-1}(X(d)^{\circ})\to X(d)^{\circ}

are a
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-gerbe and a

\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
-bundle for
N=\alpha\underline{d}
, respectively.
Note that, as we assumed
\{d\}\in S_{\delta}^{d}
,
we have
v=\langle 1_{d},\delta\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}
.
There are semiorthogonal decomposition from the projective bundle formula

(8.26)

\displaystyle D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}\circ})=\big\langle D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\circ})_{w}\,\big|\,v\leqslant w<N+v\big\rangle.

The weight
w
-part is equivalent to

D^{b}(X(d)^{\circ},\alpha^{w})
for a Brauer class
\alpha
.
Since the associated element
w\hat{\alpha}\in H^{3}(X(d)^{\circ},\mathbb{Z})
is torsion,
it does not affect the K-theory after rationalization.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}}))_{\mathbb{Q}}|_{X(d)^{\circ}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{X(d)^{\circ}}(\mathscr{X}^{{\dagger}\circ})_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\bigoplus_{w=v}^{N+v-1}\mathbb{Q}_{X(d)^{\circ}}[\beta^{\pm 1}].

On the other hand, the base change of the semiorthogonal
decomposition in Theorem
3.5
via
X(d)^{\circ}\subset X(d)

is the semiorthogonal decomposition (
8.26
), and

\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)
corresponds to
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\circ})_{v}
under the
base change. Here, we refer to Subsection
13.4

for the base change of semiorthogonal decomposition.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}|_{X(d)^{\circ}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{X(d)^{\circ}}[\beta^{\pm 1}].

By comparing with (
8.25
),
there exists
A\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
such that

P_{A}=\mathrm{IC}_{X(d)}[-1]
, i.e.
\{d\}\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
.
In particular,
we have an isomorphism of the form

(8.27)

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathrm{IC}_{X(d)}[\beta^{\pm}][1-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)]\oplus K^{\prime\prime}.

We next show that any
A\in S_{\delta}^{d}
is contained in

\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
Let
A
correspond to a partition

\mathbf{d}=\{d_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
, and
\lambda
an antidominant
cocharacter with associated partition
\mathbf{d}
.
By Proposition
8.2
, the categorical
Hall product restricts to the functor

\displaystyle\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{M}(d_{i};\theta_{i}+\delta_{i})\to\mathbb{M}(d;\delta).

The above functor is linear over
X(d)
,
so it induces the morphism in
D(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X(d)))
:

\displaystyle i_{\lambda\ast}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{K}_{X(d_{i})}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d_{i};\theta_{i}+\delta_{i}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Here we have used the notation of the diagram (
3.4
).
From Remark
8.4
, we have
\langle 1_{d_{i}},\theta_{i}+\delta_{i}\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Therefore we have the decomposition (
8.27
)
for
(d,\delta)
replaced by
(d_{i},\theta_{i}+\delta_{i})
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{ch}\colon\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d_{i};\theta_{i}+\delta_{i}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathrm{IC}_{X(d_{i})}[\beta^{\pm}][1-\dim\mathscr{X}(d_{i})]\oplus K_{i}.

We have the following diagram

\textstyle{i_{\lambda\ast}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{K}_{X(d_{i})}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d_{i};\theta_{i}+\delta_{i}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{\lambda\ast}q_{\lambda}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ch}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ch}}
\textstyle{i_{\lambda\ast}(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{IC}_{X(d_{i})}[-1])[-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}][\beta^{\pm 1}]\oplus K^{\prime\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{A^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}^{\prime}}P_{A^{\prime}}[-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)][\beta^{\pm 1}].}

Here
K^{\prime\prime\prime}=i_{\lambda\ast}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}K_{i}
.
By taking the total cohomologies, we obtain
the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(i_{\lambda\ast}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{K}_{X(d_{i})}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d_{i};\theta_{i}+\delta_{i}))_{\mathbb{Q}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{\ast}q^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces)}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\ast-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}(i_{\lambda\ast}(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{IC}_{X(d_{i})}[-1]))[\beta^{\pm 1}]\oplus\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(K^{\prime\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{A^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha}^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}^{\ast-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)}(P_{A^{\prime}})[\beta^{\pm 1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\ast-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}(i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces[\beta^{\pm 1}]}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(m_{\lambda}^{\rm{co}})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\ast-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)}(\pi_{\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)})[\beta^{\pm 1}].}

Here
the bottom vertical arrows are embeddings into
direct summands, and

m_{\lambda}^{\rm{co}}
is the
sheaf theoretic cohomological Hall algebra (
3.8
).
By
[
DM20
, Theorem C]
, the image of

i_{\lambda\ast}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{IC}_{X(d_{i})}[-1]

in
{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(\pi_{d\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{X(d)})

under

{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(m_{\lambda}^{\rm{co}})

is
P_{A}
.
Therefore, noting that

\mathcal{H}^{\ast}{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(\pi_{d\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)})=\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(\pi_{d\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)})
,
we see that the image of the bottom right arrow in the above commutative diagram
contains elements with non-zero elements from
the summand
\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(P_{A})
.
Therefore, we must have
A\in\mathscr{P}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
.

It follows that there is an isomorphism of the form

(8.28)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\bigoplus_{A\in S_{\delta}^{d}}P_{A}[\beta^{\pm 1}][1-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)]\oplus K^{\prime\prime\prime}.

By applying the functor
\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}
to (
8.28
), we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathcal{BPS}^{\rm{inv}}_{d,\delta}[\beta^{\pm 1}][-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)]\oplus\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}(K^{\prime\prime\prime}).

On the other hand, by Lemma
6.12
, there is
an isomorphism

\displaystyle\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}.

It follows that

\mathcal{BPS}^{\rm{inv}}_{d,\delta}[\beta^{\pm 1}][-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)]
is a direct
summand of
\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}
, therefore

(8.29)

\displaystyle\dim H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta})^{\rm{inv}}\leqslant\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}.

However, note that Theorem
8.15
and Lemma
8.16
imply that

\dim H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta})^{\rm{inv}}\geqslant\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Thus equality holds in (
8.29
) and the conclusion follows from Theorem
8.15
.
∎

The above proof of Theorem
8.14

also implies the following sheaf theoretic version:

Theorem 8.26
.

There is an isomorphism in
D(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X(d)))
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X(d)}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}^{\rm{inv}}[\beta^{\pm 1}][-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)].

Proof.

From the isomorphism (
8.28
)
in the proof of Theorem
8.14
, it is enough to show
that
\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}(K^{\prime\prime\prime})=0
.
The inequality (
8.29
) is indeed an equality,
thus we have
H^{\ast}(\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}(K^{\prime\prime\prime}))=0
.
Let
g\colon U\to X(d)
be an étale map.
One can apply the same argument in Theorem
8.14
to show
that the cycle map

\displaystyle c\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}_{U}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\to H^{\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-2\ell-i}(U,g^{\ast}\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}[1])

is an isomorphism.
Here
\mathscr{X}_{U}(d)=\mathscr{X}(d)\times_{X(d)}U
,
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{U}(d;\delta):=\mathrm{MF}(\mathbb{M}_{U}(d;\delta),g^{\ast}\mathop{\rm Tr}W)

where
\mathbb{M}_{U}(d;\delta)\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{U}(d))

is the base-change of
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)

under
U\to X(d)
, see Subsection
13.4
.
Then we have
H^{\ast}(g^{\ast}\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}(K^{\prime\prime\prime}))=0
.
As it holds for any étale map
g\colon U\to X(d)
,
we have that
\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}(K^{\prime\prime\prime})=0
.
∎

9.
The cycle maps on quasi-BPS categories

In this section, we construct the cycle map from topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories to BPS cohomology, see Theorem
8.15
.
The construction is based on the fact that the weight conditions for complexes in
\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)
restrict the possible perverse degree of their image under the cycle map, see Proposition
9.8
and Corollary
9.12
.
In Subsection
9.1
, we reduce the proof for a general symmetric quiver to that of a quiver with at least two loops at every vertex.
In Subsection
9.2
, we prove a restriction statement of the image under the cycle map of an object in a quasi-BPS category. In Subsection
9.3
, we combine the above restriction with the decomposition theorems (
8.19
) to prove Theorem
8.15
.

9.1.
Reduction to quivers with enough loops

In this subsection,
we reduce Theorem
8.15
to the case of quivers
with potentials where there exist at
two loops at each vertex.
Let
Q=(I,E)
be a symmetric quiver
with potential
W
.
We produce another quiver with potential

(Q^{\sharp},W^{\sharp})
as follows.
For each vertex
i\in I
, let
\omega_{i},\omega^{\prime}_{i}
be two loops at
i
.
We then set

\displaystyle E^{\sharp}:=E\sqcup\{\omega_{i},\omega^{\prime}_{i}\mid i\in I\},\ W^{q}:=\sum_{I\in I}\omega_{i}\omega^{\prime}_{i}.

The quiver with potential
(Q^{\sharp},W^{\sharp})

is defined by

Q^{\sharp}:=(I,E^{\sharp}),\,W^{\sharp}:=W+W^{q}.

Proposition 9.1
.

Assume Theorem
8.15
holds for
(Q^{\sharp},W^{\sharp})
. Then Theorem
8.15
holds for
(Q,W)
.

Recall that the stack of
Q
-representations
is given by
\mathscr{X}(d)=R(d)/G(d)
.
For the quiver
Q^{\sharp}
and for
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
,
the moduli stack of
Q^{\sharp}
-representations
and its good moduli space is given by

\displaystyle\pi^{\sharp}_{d}\colon\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d):=\left(R(d)\oplus\mathfrak{g}(d)^{\oplus 2}\right)/G(d)\to X^{\sharp}(d).

We have the
BPS sheaves
\mathcal{BPS}^{\sharp}_{d,v}
on
X^{\sharp}(d)
as defined in (
8.7
),
the polytope
\mathbb{W}^{\sharp}(d)
as in (
3.11
), the integers
n^{\sharp}_{\lambda}
as in (
3.13
), the quasi-BPS categories
\mathbb{M}^{\sharp}(d;\delta)
from (
3.12
) and
\mathbb{S}^{\sharp}(d;\delta)
from (
3.14
).
Let
\mathscr{S}(d)
be defined by

\mathscr{S}(d):=(R(d)\oplus\mathfrak{g}(d))/G(d)

and consider the following
diagram

(9.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\sharp}(d).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\sharp}_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

Here
v
is the natural projections and
s
is the natural inclusion,
the vertical maps are good moduli space morphisms and
the bottom horizontal arrow is the induced map on good moduli spaces.
We discuss some preliminary lemma and propositions.

Lemma 9.2
.

For
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
there is an isomorphism

(9.2)

\displaystyle s_{*}v^{*}\colon H^{i}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1])

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{i}(\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{\sharp}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}[-1]).

Proof.

It is enough to show the isomorphism in
D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)))
:

(9.3)

s_{*}v^{*}\colon u_{*}\pi_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1]\xrightarrow{\cong}\pi^{\sharp}_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{\sharp}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}[-1].

By the dimensional reduction isomorphism (
7.5
),
we have the isomorphism

\displaystyle i_{\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W^{q}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}[-1].

Here
i\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)

is the natural inclusion.
By applying
\pi_{d\ast}
to both sides,
we obtain the isomorphism
in
D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)))
:

(9.4)

s_{*}v^{*}\colon u_{*}\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\xrightarrow{\cong}\pi^{\sharp}_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{q}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}[-1].

By abuse of notation, we write

\mathop{\rm Tr}W\colon\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}\to\mathbb{C}
the
pull-back of
\mathop{\rm Tr}W\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\to\mathbb{C}

via the projection
\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)\to\mathscr{X}(d)
.
Note that
\pi_{d*}
commutes with
\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}
because
\pi_{d}
can be approximated with the proper maps
\pi_{\alpha f,d}
, see Subsection
8.4
. Further,
\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}
commutes with proper pushforward and smooth pullback.
By applying
\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}
to both sides of (
9.4
) and using the Thom-Sebastiani theorem for vanishing cycles, we obtain the isomorphisms

\displaystyle s_{*}v^{*}\colon u_{*}\pi_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cong}\pi^{\sharp}_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\left(\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{q}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}[-1]\right)[-1]

\displaystyle\cong\pi^{\sharp}_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{\sharp}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}[-1].

Therefore we obtain the isomorphism (
9.3
).
∎

Proposition 9.3
.

The isomorphism (
9.2
) in Lemma
9.2

restricts to the isomorphism

\displaystyle H^{i}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta})

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}H^{i}(X^{\sharp}(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{\sharp}_{d,\delta}).

Proof.

It is enough to show that the isomorphism (
9.3
) induces an isomorphism in
D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)))

u_{*}\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{BPS}^{\sharp}_{d,\delta}.

We first show that
S^{d}_{\delta}(Q)=S^{d}_{\delta}(Q^{\sharp})
.
For a cocharacter
\lambda
of
T(d)
, let
n_{\lambda}
and
n^{\sharp}_{\lambda}
be the integers (
3.13
) for
Q
and
Q^{\sharp}
, respectively. Let
\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta}
and
\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta}^{\sharp}
be the integers (
8.1
) for
Q
and
Q^{\sharp}
, respectively.
Then

n^{\sharp}_{\lambda}-n_{\lambda}=2\langle\lambda,\mathfrak{g}(d)^{\lambda>0}\rangle\in 2\mathbb{Z}.

Therefore
\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta}=\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta}^{\sharp}
, so indeed
S^{d}_{\delta}(Q)=S^{d}_{\delta}(Q^{\sharp})=:S^{d}_{\delta}
.

It suffices to check that (
9.3
) induces isomorphisms:

(9.5)

u_{*}\mathcal{BPS}_{A}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{BPS}^{\sharp}_{A}

for any
A\in S^{d}_{\delta}
.
The isomorphism (
9.3
) is obtained by applying the functor
\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}
to the isomorphism (
9.3
) for
W=0
, that is, from the isomorphism (
9.4
).
Therefore it suffices to check (
9.5
) when
W=0
, so we assume that
W=0
in the rest of the proof.
Suppose that
A
has a corresponding partition
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
of
d
. Let
X_{A}
be the image of the addition map
\oplus\colon\times_{i=1}^{k}X(d_{i})\to X(d)
.
By (
9.4
), there is an isomorphism:

u_{*}{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{k}(\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)})\xrightarrow{\sim}{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{k}(\pi^{\sharp}_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{q}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}[-1]).

There are either no summands of support
X_{A}
on both sides, case in which both
u_{*}\mathcal{BPS}_{A}
and
\mathcal{BPS}^{\sharp}_{A}
are zero, or there are unique summands of support
X_{A}
on both sides, namely
u_{*}\mathcal{BPS}_{A}
and
\mathcal{BPS}^{\sharp}_{A}
, and thus (
9.5
) follows.
∎

We note the following corollary of Lemma
9.2
and Proposition
9.3

on the isomorphism of the monodromy invariants parts:

Corollary 9.4
.

Let
\delta\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
There is an isomorphism

\displaystyle s_{*}v^{*}\colon H^{i}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1])\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{i}(\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d),\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{\sharp}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}[-1]),

which restricts to the isomorphism

\displaystyle H^{i}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{\mathrm{inv}}_{d,\delta})\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{i}(X^{\sharp}(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{\sharp,\mathrm{inv}}_{d,\delta}).

We recall the relation
between quasi-BPS categories under Knorrer periodicity:

Proposition 9.5
.

(
[
PTe
, Proposition 2.14]
)

There is an equivalence:

\displaystyle s_{*}v^{*}\colon\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{\sharp}),

which restricts to the equivalence

\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{S}^{\sharp}(d;\delta)
.

We now give a proof of Proposition
9.1
:

Proof of Proposition
9.1
.

Let
i,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By Corollary
6.10
, there is a commutative diagram, where
b=\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-i-2\ell=\dim\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)-i-2(\ell+\dim\mathfrak{g})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell+\dim\mathfrak{g}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{\sharp}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}^{\mathrm{inv}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{inv}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}^{\sharp}(d)}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{*}v^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{*}v^{*}}

The conclusion follows from Corollary
9.4
and Proposition
9.5
.
∎

9.2.
Coproduct-like maps in K-theory

By Proposition
9.1
, it is enough to consider quivers
satisfying the following assumption:

Assumption 9.1
.

Assume that the quiver
Q
is symmetric and has at least two loops at any vertex.

In this subsection, under the above assumption,
we construct coproduct-like maps in topological K-theory
of matrix factorizations.

We introduce some notation. For a cocharacter
\lambda
of
T(d)

with associated partition
\mathbf{d}
, we define

(9.6)

\displaystyle c_{\mathbf{d}}:=c_{\lambda}:=\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}.

The above assumption of the quiver is used in the following lemma:

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
Q=(I,E)
be a quiver which satisfies Assumption
9.1
and let
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
be a non-zero dimension vector.

(a) For any cocharacter
\lambda
of
T(d)
, we have
c_{\lambda}\geqslant 0
, and the inequality is strict if
\lambda
has an associated partition with at least two terms. Moreover, we have

\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}=c_{\lambda}.

(b) There exists a simple representation of dimension
d
.
In particular, the map
\pi_{d}\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\to X(d)
is generically a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-gerbe.

Proof.

Let
S(d)
be the affine space of dimension
d
representations of
the quiver obtained from
Q
by deleting one loop at every vertex in
I
.
Then

\mathscr{X}(d)=\left(S(d)\oplus\mathfrak{g}(d)\right)/G(d).

We have that

\dim\mathscr{X}(d)=\dim S(d)
and
\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}=\dim S(d)^{\geqslant 0}
.
Note that
S(d)
contains
\mathfrak{g}(d)
as a
sub
G(d)
-representation.
Therefore we have

\displaystyle c_{\lambda}=\dim S(d)^{\lambda<0}\geqslant\dim\mathfrak{g}(d)^{\lambda<0}\geqslant 0,

and
the last inequality is strict if
\lambda
corresponds
to a partition of length at least two. Therefore (a)
holds.
Then (b) holds since the union of the images of

a_{\lambda}\colon\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}\to\mathscr{X}(d)

for every cocharacters
\lambda
corresponding to
partitions of length at least two is strictly
smaller than
\mathscr{X}(d)
because of
c_{\lambda}>0
.

∎

Below we assume that
Q
satisfies Assumption
9.1
.
Let
\lambda
be an antidominant cocharacter of
T(d)
.
We have the natural
morphism
a_{\lambda}\colon\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}\to\mathscr{X}(d)

induced by the inclusion
R(d)^{\lambda}\hookrightarrow R(d)
,
see the diagram (
3.4
).
Its pull-back
and cycle maps give the following commutative
diagram

(9.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathop{\rm Tr}W))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell-2c_{\lambda}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathrm{MF}\left(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda},\mathrm{Tr}\,W\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-2\ell-i}\left(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}^{\mathrm{inv}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}-2l-i}\left(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda},\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}^{\mathrm{inv}}\right).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{*}_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{*}_{\lambda}}

Here we have used that
2c_{\lambda}:=\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}
by Lemma
9.6
.
Since
\lambda
acts on
\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}
trivially,
the stack
\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}
is a
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-gerbe
over another stack
\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}

(9.8)

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}\to\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}:=R(d)^{\lambda}/G(d)^{\prime}

where
G(d)^{\prime}:=G(d)^{\lambda}/\text{image}(\lambda)
.

Note that the relative tangent bundle of
\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}\to\mathscr{X}(d)

is
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}^{\lambda>0}
. Its determinant
line bundle descends to a line bundle
\det\left(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}^{\lambda>0}\right)

on
\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}

with
\lambda
-weight
n_{\lambda}
. Note that
n_{\lambda}>0
by
Assumption
9.1
if the associated partition
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}

has length
k\geqslant 2
.
We set

\displaystyle h:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n_{\lambda}}c_{1}(\det\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}^{\lambda>0})\in H^{2}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda},\mathbb{Q}),\text{ if }k\geqslant 2,\\
c_{1}(\tau_{d}),\text{ if }k=1.\end{cases}

We have the isomorphism

(9.9)

\displaystyle H^{\ast}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W})[h]\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}H^{\ast}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda},\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}).

We need a little more care of having such an isomorphism for
K-theory, as the
n_{\lambda}
-th root of
\det\left(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}^{\lambda>0}\right)
does not
necessary exist.
Instead we consider the following.
Let
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
be a partition associated with
\lambda
,
and consider the
subgroup
SG(d)^{\lambda}\subset G(d)^{\lambda}

defined as the kernel of the morphism

\displaystyle G(d)^{\lambda}\to\mathbb{C}^{\ast},\ (g_{i}^{a})_{a\in I,1\leqslant i\leqslant k}\mapsto\prod_{a,i}\det g_{a}^{i}.

There is a surjective group homomorphism

(9.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\times SG(d)^{\lambda}\to G(d)^{\lambda},\ (t,g)\mapsto\lambda(t)g.

The kernel of the above homomorphism is a finite
abelian group. We set

(9.11)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\lambda}:=R(d)^{\lambda}/(\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\times SG(d)^{\lambda})\to\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\prime\lambda}:=R(d)^{\lambda}/SG(d)^{\lambda}.

Here
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\times SG(d)^{\lambda}
acts on
R(d)^{\lambda}
through
the surjection (
9.10
).
The morphism (
9.11
) is a trivial
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-gerbe, so we have
the corresponding isomorphism

(9.12)

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathrm{MF}\left(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\mathrm{Tr}\,W\right)\right)[q^{\pm 1}]\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathrm{MF}\left(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\lambda},\mathrm{Tr}\,W\right)\right).

Here
q
is the class of weight one
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-character
pulled back via the projection
\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\lambda}\to B\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
.
Note that
q^{n_{\lambda}}
and the pull-back of

\det\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda>0}}
via
\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\lambda}\to\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}

only differs by a character of
SG(d)^{\lambda}
.
We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\prime\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}.}

Here each vertical arrow is a gerbe of a finite abelian group
and the horizontal arrows are
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-gerbes.
We have the following lemma.

Lemma 9.7
.

The diagram (
9.2
) induces the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda},\mathop{\rm Tr}W))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathrm{MF}\left(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\lambda},\mathrm{Tr}\,W\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\ast}\left(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}^{\mathrm{inv}}\right)[h]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\ast}\left(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}^{\mathrm{inv}}\right)[h].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Here the horizontal arrows are given by pull-backs,
and the bottom arrow is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The diagram commutes by the construction.
The bottom arrow is an isomorphism since

\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\prime\lambda}\to\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}
is a gerbe over
a finite abelian group, and because
H^{\ast}(B\mu_{d},\mathbb{Q})=\mathbb{Q}
for any
d\geqslant 1
.
∎

Recall the definition of
c_{\mathbf{d}}
and
c_{\lambda}
in (
9.6
).
We define

(9.17)

c_{\lambda,\delta}:=c_{\lambda}+\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta},\,c_{\mathbf{d},\delta}:=c_{\mathbf{d}}+\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\delta}.

We following is the main technical result of this subsection.

Proposition 9.8
.

For each partition
\mathbf{d}=(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
of
d
,
there exists an antidominant cocharacter
\lambda
of
T(d)
with
associated partition
\mathbf{d}
such that,
in the commutative diagram (
9.7
),
the image of
\mathrm{c}\,a^{*}_{\lambda}\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)\right)
lies in the subspace

\bigoplus_{j=0}^{c_{\lambda,\delta}-1}H^{2\dim\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}-2\ell-i-2j}\left(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\mathrm{inv}}\right)h^{j}\subset H^{\ast}\left(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\mathrm{inv}}\right)[h].

Moreover, the cocharacter
\lambda
satisfies
\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta}=\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\lambda}
,
hence
c_{\lambda,\delta}=c_{\mathbf{d},\lambda}
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda
be an antidominant cocharacter with corresponding
partition
\mathbf{d}
. We may take such
\lambda
such
that
\lambda\colon\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\to T(d)
is injective.
For an object
A\in\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)
,
by the definition of quasi-BPS category, the pull-back
a_{\lambda}^{*}(A)

has
\lambda
-weights contained in the interval

S_{\lambda,\delta}:=\left[-\frac{n_{\lambda}}{2}+\langle\lambda,\delta\rangle,\frac{n_{\lambda}}{2}+\langle\lambda,\delta\rangle\right]\cap\mathbb{Z}.

Thus, by setting
b_{\lambda}

to be the composition of
a_{\lambda}
with

\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\lambda}\to\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}
, we obtain

(9.18)

b^{*}_{\lambda}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)\right)\subset K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathrm{MF}\left(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\mathrm{Tr}\,W\right)\right)\otimes\mathscr{A},

where

\mathscr{A}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}:=\bigoplus_{j\in S_{\lambda,\delta}}\mathbb{Q}\cdot q^{j}\subset K_{0}^{\rm{top}}(B\mathbb{C}^{\ast})=\mathbb{Q}[q^{\pm 1}].

There
exists a filtration in the right hand side
of (
9.18
)
induced by the Chern character maps for both
K_{i}^{\mathrm{top}}\left(\mathrm{MF}(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\mathrm{Tr}\,W)\right)
and
K_{0}^{\mathrm{top}}\left(B\mathbb{C}^{*}\right)
, and there is an isomorphism obtained by the Kunneth formula:

(9.19)

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K_{i}^{\mathrm{top}}\left(\mathrm{MF}(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\lambda},\mathrm{Tr}\,W)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{a+b=\ell}\mathrm{gr}_{a}K_{i}^{\mathrm{top}}\left(\mathrm{MF}(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\mathrm{Tr}\,W)\right)\otimes\mathrm{gr}_{b}K_{0}^{\mathrm{top}}\left(B\mathbb{C}^{*}\right).

The filtration on
\mathcal{A}
is induced by the filtration on

\mathbb{Q}[q^{\pm 1}]
given by

(q-1)^{b}\mathbb{Q}[q^{\pm 1}]\subset\mathbb{Q}[q^{\pm 1}]

for
b\geqslant 0
, see Example
5.5
.
Note that we have

(9.20)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}\cap(q-1)^{b}\mathbb{Q}[q^{\pm 1}]=0,\ b\geqslant n_{\lambda}+\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta}.

Suppose that
k=2
, i.e.
\mathbf{d}
is a
two length partition
d=d_{1}+d_{2}
.
In this case, we choose
\lambda

which acts on
\beta^{i}_{a}
for
i\in I
with weight one
for
1\leqslant a\leqslant d^{i}_{1}
and zero for
a>d^{i}_{1}
.
Then the
\lambda
-weights on
R(d)
and
\mathfrak{g}(d)

are either zero or
\pm 1
, so we have
n_{\lambda}=c_{\lambda}
.
Therefore the conclusion follows from (
9.20
)
and Lemma
9.7
.

Suppose that
k\geqslant 3
. The stack
\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}

only depends on a partition
\bf{d}
, so we may write it as

\mathscr{X}(\mathbf{d})=R(\mathbf{d})/G(\mathbf{d})
. It is a
(\mathbb{C}^{\ast})^{k}
-gerbe

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}(\mathbf{d})\to\mathscr{X}(\mathbf{d})^{\prime}=R(\mathbf{d})/G(\mathbf{d})^{\prime}

where
G(\mathbf{d})^{\prime}=G(\mathbf{d})/Z(\mathbf{d})

and
Z(\mathbf{d})=(\mathbb{C}^{\ast})^{k}\subset T(d_{1})\times\cdots\times T(d_{k})
is the diagonal
torus.
As it is not necessarily a trivial gerbe, we apply a similar construction
as in (
9.11
).
By setting
S(\mathbf{d})=\times_{i=1}^{k}SG(d_{i})
,
we have the surjection
Z(\mathbf{d})\times S(\mathbf{d})\to G(\mathbf{d})
,
and trivial
Z(\mathbf{d})
-gerbe

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(\mathbf{d}):=R(\mathbf{d})/(Z(\mathbf{d})\times S(\mathbf{d}))\to\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(\mathbf{d})^{\prime}:=R(\mathbf{d})/S(\mathbf{d}).

Similarly to (
9.12
), we have the isomorphism

\displaystyle K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathrm{MF}(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(\mathbf{d})^{\prime},\mathop{\rm Tr}W))_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}[q_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,q_{k}^{\pm 1}]\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathrm{MF}(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(\mathbf{d}),\mathop{\rm Tr}W))_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Giving an antidominant cocharacter of
T(d)
with corresponding partition
\mathbf{d}

is equivalent to giving an element

(9.21)

\displaystyle\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{k})\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\ast},Z(\mathbf{d}))=\mathbb{Z}^{k},\ \lambda_{1}>\cdots>\lambda_{k}.

Let
\gamma
be the composition

\gamma\colon\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(\mathbf{d})\to\mathscr{X}(\mathbf{d})\to\mathscr{X}(d)
.
We need to find such
\lambda
such that
the vanishing orders of the elements

(9.22)

\displaystyle\gamma^{\ast}K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))\subset K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathrm{MF}(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}(\mathbf{d})^{\prime},\mathop{\rm Tr}W))\otimes\mathbb{Q}[q_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,q_{k}^{\pm 1}]

at the hypersurface

\displaystyle F_{\lambda}:=(f_{\lambda}=0)\subset Z(\mathbf{d}),\ f_{\lambda}:=q_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots q_{k}^{\lambda_{k}}-1

are
at most
c_{\lambda,\delta}-1
.
By the argument of the length two partition case,
by setting
\lambda_{0}=(1,0,\cdots,0)

the vanishing orders of the elements (
9.22
) at
F_{\lambda_{0}}=\{q_{1}=1\}
are at most
c_{\lambda_{0},\delta}-1
.
We then perturb
\lambda_{0}
to a rational
cocharacter of
Z(\mathbf{d})
,

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}=(1,\lambda_{2}^{\prime},\cdots,\lambda_{k}^{\prime}),\ 1\gg\lambda_{2}^{\prime}>\cdots>\lambda_{k}^{\prime}

where
\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Q}
. By a generic perturbation, the elements
from the left hand side of (
9.18
) are divisible by

f_{\lambda^{\prime}}
at most
(c_{\lambda_{0},\delta}-1)
-times.

Indeed, the locus in the left hand side in (
9.18
)
divisible
m
-times by
(q_{1}-1)
is given by
the intersection
H_{1}\cap\cdots\cap H_{m}
,
where
H_{i}

is the linear subspace determined by the condition

(\partial/\partial q_{1})^{i-1}(-)|_{q_{1}=1}=0
.
Then we have
H_{1}\cap\cdots\cap H_{c_{\lambda_{0},\delta}}=0
.
By a small perturbation of
\lambda_{0}
,
the locus divisible
m
-times by
f_{\lambda^{\prime}}
is the intersection
H_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap H_{m}^{\prime}
,
where
H_{i}^{\prime}
is a perturbation of
H_{i}
.
Therefore, we have
H_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap H_{c_{\lambda_{0},\delta}}^{\prime}=0

for an enough small perturbation of
\lambda_{0}
.

We then take
N>0
such that
\lambda=N\lambda^{\prime}
is integral.
Since the vanishing orders at
F_{\lambda^{\prime}}
and
F_{N\lambda^{\prime}}
are
the same and we have the inequality
c_{\lambda_{0}}<c_{\lambda}
,
we obtain a desired cocharacter
\lambda
.
Moreover, the above choice of
\lambda
can be taken
arbitrary of the form (
9.21
)
such that
\lambda_{1}\gg\lambda_{2}>\cdots>\lambda_{k}
,
which are general enough to assure that

\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta}=\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\delta}
.
∎

9.3.
Coproduct-like maps in cohomology

In this subsection, we
construct coproduct-like maps in cohomologies and
use them together with Proposition
9.8
to give a proof of
Theorem
8.15
.
Below we
assume that the quiver
Q=(I,E)
satisfies Assumption
9.1
.
By Proposition
9.1
, we obtain Theorem
8.15
for general symmetric quivers
Q
.

For
\lambda
an antidominant cocharacter with associated partition
\mathbf{d}=(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
,
let
\pi_{\lambda}
be the good moduli space map

\displaystyle\pi_{\lambda}:=\times_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{d_{i}}\colon\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}\to X(d)^{\lambda}:=\times_{i=1}^{k}X(d_{i}).

Let
t_{\lambda}
be the projection
t_{\lambda}\colon\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}\to\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}
,
which is a
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-gerbe. The maps introduced fit in the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{a_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)^{{}^{\prime}\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{X(d)^{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{d}}
\textstyle{X(d).}

We consider the following perverse truncation

(9.23)

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}\colon t_{\lambda*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}[c_{\lambda}]

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}}[c_{\lambda}-1]\otimes\mathbb{Q}[h]

\displaystyle\to{}^{p}\tau^{\geqslant c_{\lambda}+1}\left(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}}[c_{\lambda}-1]\otimes\mathbb{Q}[h]\right)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{j\geqslant 0}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}}[-c_{\lambda}-1-2j]\cong t_{\lambda*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}[-c_{\lambda}].

The map
\mathrm{S}
sends
h^{i}
for
0\leqslant i\leqslant c_{\lambda}-1
to zero,
and
h^{i}
for
i\geqslant c_{\lambda}
to
h^{i-c_{\lambda}}
.
We then define the map
\Delta_{\lambda}
to be the composition

(9.24)

\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\pi_{d\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\to\pi_{d\ast}a_{\lambda\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}[2c_{\lambda}]=i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}[2c_{\lambda}]\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}.

Recall the notations from Subsection
8.4
and the decomposition theorem (
8.19
).
We consider the total perverse cohomology

\displaystyle{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}\left(\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\right):=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{i}\left(\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\right)[-i].

For
A\in\mathscr{P}
as in Subsection
8.4
,
we have natural maps into and onto the direct summand
\mathrm{P}_{A}

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{A}\hookrightarrow{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}\left(\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\right)\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{P}_{A}.

Suppose that the corresponding partition of
A
is
\mathbf{d}=(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}

as above, and let

B\in\mathscr{P}
be another element.
We have the following map

\displaystyle\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{B}}\hookrightarrow{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}\left(\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\right)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}\left(\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}\right)\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{P}_{A}.

Proposition 9.9
.

Let
A,B\in\mathscr{P}
with corresponding sheaves
\mathrm{P}_{A}
and
\mathrm{P}_{B}
of different support. Assume that
p_{B}\leqslant p_{A}
.

(a) The map (
9.24
) induces an isomorphism

(9.25)

\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{P}_{A}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathrm{P}_{A}.

(b) The map
\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{P}_{B}\to\mathrm{P}_{A}
is zero.

Proof.

(a)
Recall the subset
\mathscr{P}^{\circ}\subset\mathscr{P}
in Subsection
8.5
.
We first prove the case that
A\in\mathscr{P}^{\circ}
, i.e.

A
corresponds to a tuple
(e_{i},m_{i,a})_{i=1}^{s}
such that

m_{i,a}=0
for
a\geqslant 1
.
Let
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
be a partition of
d

where
e_{j}
appear
m_{j}:=m_{j,0}
-times, and
\lambda

an antidominant cocharacter with associated partition
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
.
Recall the sheaf theoretic cohomological Hall algebra (
3.8
)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda}^{\rm{co}}\colon i_{\lambda\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}\to\pi_{d\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}.

The lowest non-zero piece of the perverse filtration on
i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}
is given by

{}^{p}\tau^{\leqslant k}i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}=i_{\lambda\ast}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{s}\left(\mathrm{IC}_{X(e_{i})}[-1]\right)^{\boxtimes m_{i}}.

The (shifted) perverse sheaf
i_{\lambda\ast}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{s}\left(\mathrm{IC}_{X(e_{i})}[-1]\right)^{\boxtimes m_{i}}
splits as a direct sum of simple perverse sheaves, and one of
them is
\mathrm{P}_{A}
.
There is thus a natural inclusion
\mathrm{P}_{A}\subset i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}
.
The map

(9.26)

\Delta_{\lambda}m_{\lambda}^{\rm{co}}\colon\mathrm{P}_{A}\to i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}

has image in the lowest non-zero perverse truncation of
i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}
,
and thus (
9.26
) induces a map:

(9.27)

\Delta_{\lambda}m_{\lambda}^{\rm{co}}\colon\mathrm{P}_{A}\to{}^{p}\tau^{\leqslant s}i_{\lambda\ast}\pi_{\lambda*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}=\boxtimes_{i=1}^{s}\left(\mathrm{IC}_{X(e_{i})}[-1]\right)^{\boxtimes m_{i}}.

The (shifted) perverse sheaf
i_{\lambda\ast}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\left(\mathrm{IC}_{X(e_{i})}[-1]\right)^{\boxtimes m_{i}}
has only one summand isomorphic to
\mathrm{P}_{A}
, which is a simple (shifted) perverse sheaf. Thus the map (
9.27
) restricts to a map

(9.28)

\mathrm{P}_{A}\to\mathrm{P_{A}}

All such maps are given by multiplication by scalars. It is thus an isomorphism if it is not the zero map. It suffices to show that the maps (
9.26
) or (
9.27
) are not zero. We will show this after passing to a convenient open set of
X(d)^{\lambda}
.

For any non-zero
e\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
, by the same argument used to prove Lemma
9.6
, there exists a point in
R(e)
corresponding to a simple representation.
For
1\leqslant i\leqslant k
, let
R_{i}
be a simple representation of
Q
of dimension
d_{i}
such that
R_{i}
and
R_{j}
are not isomorphic for
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant k
. Let
R:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}R_{i}
. Note that the stabilizer of
R
is
T=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}
. By the etale slice theorem, there is an analytic smooth open substack
R\in\mathscr{U}/T\subset\mathscr{X}(d)
such that

\mathscr{U}/\!\!/T\to X(d)\text{ and }\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/\!\!/T\to X(d)^{\lambda}

are analytic neighborhoods of
\pi_{d}(R)
and
\times_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{d_{i}}(R_{i})=\pi_{\lambda}(R)
, respectively. After possibly shrinking
\mathscr{U}
, we may assume that
\mathscr{U}
and
\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}
are contractible. The maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda\geqslant 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(d)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\lambda}}

are, analytically locally over
\pi_{d}(R)\in X(d)
, isomorphic to the following:

(9.29)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}^{\lambda\geqslant 0}/T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}/T.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\lambda}}

Note that the maps
p_{\lambda}
and
a_{\lambda}
in (
9.29
) are closed immersions.
To show that the map (
9.28
) is non-zero, it suffices to check that the map

(9.30)

\Delta_{\lambda}m_{\lambda}|_{\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/\!\!/T}\colon\mathrm{P}_{A}|_{\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/\!\!/T}\to\mathrm{P}_{A}|_{\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/\!\!/T}

is non-zero. It suffices to check that the map is non-zero after passing to global sections. We drop the restriction to
\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}
from the notation from now on.
We check by a direct computation that

(9.31)

\Delta_{\lambda}m_{\lambda}(1)=1\in H^{0}(\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/T).

Note that the computation (
9.31
) shows that the map (
9.30
) is non-zero, and thus the conclusion follows. It suffices to check the computation in (
9.31
) for
\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/\mathbb{C}^{*}
, where by
\mathbb{C}^{*}
we denote the image of
\lambda
, because
H^{0}(\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/\mathbb{C}^{*})\cong H^{0}(\mathscr{U}^{T}/T)\cong\mathbb{Q}
. Note that we have
H^{\ast}(\mathscr{U}^{\lambda}/\mathbb{C}^{*})\cong\mathbb{Q}[h]
and that

m_{\lambda}(1)=p_{\lambda*}q_{\lambda}^{*}(1)=h^{c_{\lambda}}

because
p_{\lambda}
has relative dimension
-c_{\lambda}
. Note that
\Delta_{\lambda}(h^{c_{\lambda}})=1
from the construction of
\Delta_{\lambda}
, and thus the conclusion follows.

We next reduce the case of
A\in\mathscr{P}\setminus\mathscr{P}^{\circ}

to the case as above.
Let
A\in\mathscr{P}
corresponds to
a tuple
(e_{i},m_{i,a})
and let
A^{\circ}\in\mathscr{P}^{\circ}
be
a tuple
(e_{i},m_{i,a}^{\prime})
with
m_{i,0}^{\prime}=\sum_{a}m_{i,a}
and
m_{i,a}^{\prime}=0
for
a\geqslant 1
.
For
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
, we set
\hbar_{d}:=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(\sigma_{d}))\in H^{2}(\mathscr{X}(d))
.
By
[
DM20
, Theorem C]
,
the summand
\mathrm{P}_{A}
of
{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}\left(\pi_{d*}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\right)
is obtained from
\mathrm{P}_{A^{\circ}}

by multiplication with

u:=\mathrm{Sym}(\hbar_{d_{1}}^{b_{1}}\cdots\hbar_{d_{k}}^{b_{k}})
.
Here
b_{i}
is the sum of
a\geqslant 0
for which

m_{j,a}\neq 0
and
e_{j}=b_{i}
, and
\mathrm{Sym}(-)
is the symmetrizer with respect to the

\mathfrak{S}_{k}
-action on
(1,\ldots,k)
.
Then we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{P_{A^{\circ}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{P_{A^{\circ}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{P_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{P_{A}.}

In order to show that the above diagram commutes, by simpleness of
P_{A}
it is
enough to check at
\mathcal{U}/\!\!/T
as in the above arguments.
Then the above digram commutes by

\Delta_{\lambda}(h^{c_{\lambda}}u)=\Delta_{\lambda}(h^{c_{\lambda}})=u

from the definition of
\Delta_{\lambda}
.

(b) If
p_{B}<p_{A}
, the map
\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{P}_{B}\to\mathrm{P}_{A}
is zero by considering the perverse degree. If
p_{B}=p_{A}
, then the map is zero because, after a shift, it is a map of simple perverse sheaves with different support.
∎

Example 9.10
.

In Example
8.20
,
let
\lambda\colon\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\to(\mathbb{C}^{\ast})^{2}=T(2)

be an antidominant cocharacter
t\mapsto(t^{\lambda_{1}},t^{\lambda_{2}})

for
\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}
.
When
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}
, we have
h=\hbar_{1}-\hbar_{2}
,
c_{\lambda}=2e
and

\displaystyle\Delta_{\lambda}((h_{1}-h_{2})^{2e})(\hbar_{1}^{m_{1}}\hbar_{2}^{m_{2}}+\hbar_{1}^{m_{2}}\hbar_{2}^{m_{1}})=\hbar_{1}^{m_{1}}\hbar_{2}^{m_{2}}+\hbar_{1}^{m_{2}}\hbar_{2}^{m_{1}},

and
\Delta_{\lambda}((\hbar_{1}-\hbar_{2})^{2a})=0
for
\ 0\leqslant a\leqslant e-1
.

When
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}
, we have
h=(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})/2
,
c_{\lambda}=0
and

\displaystyle\Delta_{\lambda}((\hbar_{1}-\hbar_{2})^{a}(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})^{k})=(\hbar_{1}-\hbar_{2})(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})^{k}

for
0\leqslant a\leqslant e-1
and
k\geqslant 0
.

We next prove an analogue of Proposition
9.9
for a non-zero potential.
Let
W
be a potential of
Q
. Recall the shifted perverse sheaves
\mathrm{Q}_{A}
from Subsection
8.4
. Let
{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1])
be the total perverse cohomology of
\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1]
.
We have natural maps into and onto the
direct summands

\mathrm{Q}_{A}\hookrightarrow{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{\ast}(\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1])\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Q}_{A}.

By applying the vanishing cycle functor to the maps (
9.24
) to obtain:

(9.32)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\pi_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1]

\displaystyle\to i_{\lambda*}\pi_{\lambda*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}[-1]

\displaystyle=i_{\lambda*}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\left(\pi_{d_{i}*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d_{i})}[-1]\right).

Let
\mathrm{Q}_{A}^{\mathrm{inv}}
be defined by the exact triangle

\mathrm{Q}_{A}^{\mathrm{inv}}[-1]\to\mathrm{Q}_{A}\xrightarrow{1-\mathrm{T}}\mathrm{Q}_{A}\to\mathrm{Q}_{A}^{\mathrm{inv}}.

Proposition 9.11
.

Let
A,B\in\mathscr{P}
with corresponding perverse sheaves
\mathrm{Q}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Q}_{B}
such that
P_{A}
and
P_{B}
have different supports. Assume that
p_{B}\leqslant p_{A}
.

(a) The map (
9.32
) induces isomorphisms

(9.33)

\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{Q}_{A}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Q}_{A},\,\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{Q}^{\mathrm{inv}}_{A}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Q}^{\mathrm{inv}}_{A}.

(b) The maps
\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{Q}_{B}\to\mathrm{Q}_{A}
and
\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{Q}^{\mathrm{inv}}_{B}\to\mathrm{Q}^{\mathrm{inv}}_{A}
are zero.

Proof.

The maps above are induced from the map (
9.24
), thus the conclusion follows from Proposition
9.9
.
∎

We now record a corollary to be used in the proof of Theorem
8.15
.
Fix a splitting

(9.34)

H^{\bullet}\left(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1]\right)=\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{P}}H^{\bullet}(X(d),\mathrm{Q}^{\mathrm{inv}}_{A}).

For an element
x\in H^{\bullet}\left(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1]\right)
, using the decomposition above we write

(9.35)

\displaystyle x=\sum_{A\in\mathscr{P}}x_{A},\ x_{A}\in H^{\bullet}(X(d),\mathrm{Q}^{\mathrm{inv}}_{A}).

Corollary 9.12
.

Let
\delta\in M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}^{W_{d}}

and
\lambda
be an antidominant cocharacter of
T(d)
with associated partition
\mathbf{d}
such that
\varepsilon_{\lambda,\delta}=\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\delta}
.
For
x\in H^{i}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1])
, suppose that

(9.36)

\displaystyle a^{*}_{\lambda}(x)\in\bigoplus_{j=0}^{c_{\mathbf{d},\delta}-1}H^{i-2j}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}}[-2])h^{j}.

Then we have the followings:

(a) If
\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\delta}=0
, then
x_{A}=0
for all tuples
A\in\mathscr{P}
with corresponding partition

\mathbf{d}
.

(b) If
\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\delta}=1
, then
x_{A}=0
for all tuples
A\in\mathscr{P}
with corresponding partition

\mathbf{d}
such that
A\neq A^{\circ}
.

Proof.

If
\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\delta}=0
,
then
\mathrm{S}a_{\lambda}^{\ast}(x)=0

by the definition of the map
\mathrm{S}
in (
9.23
)
and the condition (
9.36
).
Then we have
\Delta_{\lambda}(x)=0
.
If
\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\delta}=1
,
then from the definition of
\mathrm{S}
in (
9.23
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}a_{\lambda}^{*}(x)\in H^{\ast}\left(X(d)^{\lambda},\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}^{\mathrm{inv}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}}[-c_{\lambda}-2]\right).

Therefore

\Delta_{\lambda}(x)
lies in the image of

\displaystyle H^{i}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda},\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\prime\lambda}}[-2])\hookrightarrow H^{i}(\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda},\varphi^{\mathrm{inv}}_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)^{\lambda}}[-1]).

Now both claims follow from Proposition
9.11

using the induction on
p_{A}
.
∎

Finally in this section, we give a proof of Theorem
8.15
.

Proof of Theorem
8.15
.

Recall the cycle map in (
8.9
)

\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{a}K_{i}^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))\to H^{\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-2a-i}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}^{\rm{inv}}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}).

By Proposition
9.1
, we may assume that
Q
has at least two loops at every vertex.
Let
y
be in the image of the above map.
By Proposition
9.8
and Corollary
9.12
,
we have that
y_{A}=0
unless
A=A^{\circ}
for some partition
\mathbf{d}=(d_{i},m_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
of
d
with
m_{i}\geqslant 1
and
d_{i}
pairwise distinct with
\varepsilon_{\mathbf{d},\delta}=1
. The statement thus follows.
∎

10.
Topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories for preprojective algebras

In this section, we use the results of Sections
7
and
8
to compute the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories for
preprojective algebras
in terms of BPS cohomology, see Theorem
10.6
. The results in this section can be applied (in conjunction with those from Subsection
12
) to compute the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories for local Calabi-Yau surfaces, see
[
PTd
]
.

10.1.
The preprojective BPS sheaf

Let
Q^{\circ}=(I,E^{\circ})
be a quiver
and let
(Q,W)
be its tripled quiver.
Recall the moduli stack of
Q
-representations of
dimension
d
and its good moduli space

\pi_{X,d}:=\pi_{d}\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\to X(d).

Recall also the moduli stack of representations
of the preprojective algebra of
Q^{\circ}
:

\pi_{P,d}\colon\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{cl}}\to P(d).

We consider the moduli stack of dimension
d
representations of the double quiver of
Q^{\circ}
and its good moduli space:

\pi_{Y,d}\colon\mathscr{Y}(d):=(R^{\circ}(d)\oplus R^{\circ}(d)^{\vee})/G(d)\to Y(d).

We have the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Y}(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(d).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{P,d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Y,d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X,d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

Here
\eta\colon\mathscr{X}(d)\to\mathscr{Y}(d)
is the projection which forgets the
\mathfrak{g}(d)
-component and the bottom horizontal
arrows are induced maps on good moduli spaces.
Let
\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}(d)

be the diagonal embedding, which induces the closed immersion

\displaystyle\gamma\colon X^{\prime}(d):=(R^{\circ}(d)\oplus R^{\circ}(d)^{\vee}\oplus\mathbb{C})/\!\!/G(d)\hookrightarrow X(d).

Let
\eta^{\prime}:=\eta|_{X^{\prime}(d)}
.
By
[
Dava
, Theorem/Definition 4.1]
, there exists a
preprojective BPS sheaf

\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d}\in\mathrm{Perv}(P(d))

such that the BPS sheaf of the tripled quiver with potential
(Q,W)
associated to
Q^{\circ}
is

(10.1)

\mathcal{BPS}_{d}=\gamma_{\ast}\eta^{\prime\ast}j_{*}(\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d})[1]\in\mathrm{Perv}(X(d)).

For a partition
A=(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
of
d
, define
\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{A}\in\mathrm{Perv}(P(d))
as in (
8.2
)
using addition maps on
P(d)
.
For
\delta\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}
,
define the following perverse sheaves on
P(d)
:

(10.2)

\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{\delta}:=\bigoplus_{A\in S^{d}_{\delta}}\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{A},\,\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,v}:=\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{v\tau_{d}},

where the set of partitions
S^{d}_{\delta}
is defined from the tripled quiver
Q
, see Subsection
8.1
.
Then
\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,v}
is a direct summand of
\pi_{P,d*}\omega_{\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}}}
, see
[
Dava
, Theorem A]
, and so
H^{-a}(P(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,v})
is a direct summand of

H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{a}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}})=H^{-a}\big(P(d),\pi_{P,d*}\omega_{\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}}}\big).

Recall the maps

\mathscr{P}(d)\xleftarrow{\eta^{\prime}}\eta^{-1}(\mathscr{P}(d))\xrightarrow{j^{\prime}}\mathscr{X}(d).

The dimension of
\mathscr{P}(d)
as a quasi-smooth stack is
\dim\mathscr{P}(d):=\dim\mathscr{Y}(d)-\dim\mathfrak{g}(d)
.
Recall the dimensional reduction isomorphism from Subsection
7.1
:

\displaystyle j^{\prime}_{*}\eta^{\prime*}\colon H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{a}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}})\cong H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{a}(\mathscr{P}(d))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2\dim\mathscr{Y}(d)-a}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1])

\displaystyle=H^{\dim\mathscr{P}(d)-a}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}[-1]).

By the construction of the PBW isomorphism for preprojective Hall algebras
[
Dava
, Equation (31)]
,
the above isomorphism preserves the BPS cohomologies:

(10.3)

j^{\prime}_{*}\eta^{\prime*}\colon H^{-a}(P(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,v})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\dim\mathscr{P}(d)-a}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,v}).

10.2.
Computations

Recall the categories

\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d))\text{ and }\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}}\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}})

from Subsection
3.8
.
Recall also that
we have natural closed immersions

\displaystyle l\colon\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{cl}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{red}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathscr{P}(d).

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
Q^{\circ}=(I,E^{\circ})
be a quiver, let
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
, and let
\delta\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
The closed immersion
l^{\prime}\colon\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{P}(d)
induces an equivalence of spectra:

l^{\prime}_{*}\colon K^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}})\xrightarrow{\sim}K^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)).

Proof.

There is an equivalence of spectra
l^{\prime}_{*}\colon G^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}})\xrightarrow{\sim}G^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathscr{P}(d))
, see the isomorphism (
5.2
). The claim follows from Proposition
3.4
and Theorem
3.5
.
∎

For
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, consider the Chern character map (
5.16
) for the quasi-smooth stack
\mathscr{P}(d)
:

(10.4)

\mathrm{ch}\colon G^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathscr{P}(d)\right)\to\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}_{i}(\mathscr{P}(d)).

It induces a Chern character map:

(10.5)

\mathrm{ch}\colon K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))\hookrightarrow G^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathscr{P}(d)\right)\to\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}_{i}(\mathscr{P}(d)).

Corollary 10.2
.

The map (
10.5
) is injective over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

The injectivity of (
10.5
) follows from Proposition
6.11
, Theorem
3.5
, and the Koszul equivalence (
2.11
).
∎

Corollary 10.3
.

We have
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{1}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}=0
.

Proof.

We have that
H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\mathrm{odd}}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}})=0
by
[
Davb
, Theorem A]
. The conclusion follows by Proposition
10.2
.
∎

Recall the filtration
E_{\ell}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d))
of
G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d))
from Subsection
5.6
. Define the filtration:

E_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)):=E_{\ell}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d))\cap K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))\subset K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)).

We denote by
\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))
the associated graded piece, and note that it is a direct summand of
\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d))
by Theorem
3.5
.
Similarly, we define filtrations

\displaystyle E_{\ell}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}})\subset G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{red}}),\ E_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}})\subset K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}}).

Recall the Koszul equivalence (
3.15
) and the
forget-the-grading functor

\displaystyle D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d))\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathrm{MF}^{\rm{gr}}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathop{\rm Tr}W)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathop{\rm Tr}W).

The above functors restrict to functors

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathbb{S}^{\rm{gr}}(d;\delta)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathbb{S}(d;\delta).

Corollary 10.4
.

The functor
\Theta
induces
an isomorphism:

(10.6)

\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}\left(\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W)\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}\left(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W)\right).

There are thus also isomorphisms:

\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}\left(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{gr}_{\ell+\dim\mathfrak{g}(d)}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{gr}}(d;\delta)\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{gr}_{\ell+\dim\mathfrak{g}(d)+1}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}\left(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta)\right).

Proof.

The isomorphism (
10.6
) follows from Corollaries
7.6
and
10.3
. The other isomorphism follow from the Koszul equivalence, see Proposition
7.6
for an explanation of the degree of the graded pieces.
∎

Corollary 10.5
.

There is a commutative diagram,
where the vertical maps are cycle maps and the left horizontal maps are the dimensional reduction maps
i^{\prime}_{*}p^{\prime*}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ast}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ast}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ast}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{0}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}[-1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{0}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}^{\mathrm{inv}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here we have suppressed the cohomological degrees to make the diagram simpler.

Proof.

The claim follows from Proposition
7.6
and Corollary
10.4
.
∎

Theorem 10.6
.

For any quiver
Q^{\circ}
, the cycle map (
10.4
) for
\mathscr{P}(d)
induces
an isomorphism

(10.7)

\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}H^{-2\ell}(P(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,\delta}).

Proof.

The isomorphism
\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))\cong\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)^{\mathrm{red}})
follows from Proposition
10.1
.
Consider the diagram, whose lower square commutes from Corollary
10.5
and the top horizontal map is an isomorphism by Corollary
10.4
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell+\dim\mathfrak{g}(d)+1}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{S}(d;\delta))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(d))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{\ell+\dim\mathfrak{g}(d)+1}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathrm{Tr}\,W))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{2\ell}(\mathscr{P}(d))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\dim\mathscr{Y}(d)-2\ell}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}[-1]).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

By Theorem
8.15
,
the map
\beta
has image in

H^{\dim\mathscr{P}(d)-2\ell}(\mathscr{X}(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,v})\subset H^{2\dim\mathscr{Y}(d)-2\ell}(\mathscr{X}(d),\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}[-1]).

Therefore by (
10.3
), the map
\alpha
has image in

(10.8)

\displaystyle H^{-2\ell}(P(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,\delta})\subset H_{2d}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathscr{P}(d)).

It is an isomorphism over
\mathbb{Q}
onto the left hand side of
(
10.8
) by Theorem
8.14
.
∎

Remark 10.7
.

There are two perverse filtrations on
H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\ast}(\mathscr{P}(d))
for any quiver
Q^{\circ}
. One of them is induced from the tripled quiver with potential
(Q,W)
and studied in
[
DM20
]
; the first non-zero piece in the perverse filtration is
{}^{p}\tau^{\leqslant 1}\pi_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}=\mathcal{BPS}_{d}
.
Another filtration is induced from the map
\pi_{P,d}
and studied in
[
Dava
]
, where it is called “the less perverse filtration”; the first non-zero piece in the perverse filtration is
{}^{p}\tau^{\leqslant 0}\pi_{P,d*}\omega_{\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}}}
.
Note that, for any
v\in\mathbb{Z}
,

{}^{p}\tau^{\leqslant 1}\pi_{d*}\varphi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}
is a direct summand of

\mathcal{BPS}_{d,v}
, which itself is a direct summand of
{}^{p}\tau^{\leqslant 0}\pi_{P,d*}\omega_{\mathscr{P}(d)^{\mathrm{cl}}}
. Thus the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories lies between the first non-zero pieces of these two perverse filtrations.

Remark 10.8
.

Davison–Hennecart–Schlegel Mejia computed in
[
DHSMb
,
DHSMa
]
the preprojective BPS sheaves in terms of the intersection complexes of the varieties
P(d)
.

We note the following numerical corollary of Theorem
10.6
.

Corollary 10.9
.

Let
Q^{\circ}=(I,E^{\circ})
be a quiver, let
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
, and let
\delta\in M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}^{W_{d}}
.
There is an equality

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(d;\delta))_{\mathbb{Q}}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}H^{\ast}(P(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta}).

Proof.

The map (
10.5
) is injective by Proposition
10.2
.
The conclusion then follows from Theorem
10.6
.
∎

11.
Examples

In this section, we discuss some explicit examples of computations of the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories. All vector spaces considered in this section are
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces.
We first note a preliminary proposition.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
Q=(I,E)
be a symmetric quiver, let
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
, and let
v\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then

\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}=\#\left(M(d)^{+}\cap(\mathbf{W}(d)+v\tau_{d}-\rho)\right).

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{X}(d)\to BGL(d)
is an affine fibration,
there is a natural isomorphism

K_{0}(\mathscr{X}(d))\xrightarrow{\sim}K_{0}^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathscr{X}(d))\cong K_{0}(BG(d)).

The category
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}
is admissible in
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))
, so the above isomorphism restricts
to the isomorphism

K_{0}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v})\xrightarrow{\sim}K_{0}^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}).

The basis of
K_{0}(\mathscr{X}(d))
is given by
the classes of the vector bundles
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}\otimes\Gamma_{G(d)}(\chi)
, where
\chi
is a dominant weight of
T(d)
and
\Gamma_{G(d)}(\chi)
is the irreducible representation of
G(d)
of highest weight
\chi
. The computation

\dim K_{0}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}=\#\left(M(d)^{+}\cap(\textbf{W}(d)+v\tau_{d}-\rho)\right)

follows then from the definition of
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}
.
∎

Remark 11.2
.

In view of Proposition
11.1
and Theorem
8.18
, the total intersection cohomology of the spaces
X(d)
can be determined by counting lattice points inside the polytope
(\textbf{W}(d)+v\tau_{d}-\rho)
.

11.1.
Toric examples

Let
g\in\mathbb{N}
.
Consider the quiver
Q=(I,E)
, where
I=\{1,2\}
and
E
has one loop at
1
, one loop at
2
,
2g+1
edges {
e_{1},\ldots,e_{2g+1}\}
from
0
to
1
and
2g+1
edges
\{\overline{e}_{1},\ldots,\overline{e}_{2g+1}\}
from
1
to
0
. The following is a figure for
g=1
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{e}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{e}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{e}_{3}}

Fix the dimension vector
d=(1,1)\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
.
Then

\mathscr{X}(d)=\left(\mathbb{C}^{2}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2(2g+1)}\right)\big/(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2}.

The diagonal
\mathbb{C}^{*}\hookrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2}
acts trivially on
\mathbb{C}^{2}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2(2g+1)}
. The factor
\mathbb{C}^{*}
corresponding to the vertex
1
acts with weight
0
on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
, weight
1
on
\mathbb{C}^{2g+1}
, and weight
-1
on
\mathbb{C}^{2g+1}
. We consider the stack, which is the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-rigidification of
\mathscr{X}(d)
:

\mathscr{X}^{\prime}(d)=\left(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2g+1}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2g+1}_{-1}\right)\big/\mathbb{C}^{*}.

The GIT quotient for any non-trivial stability condition provides a small resolution of singularities:

\tau\colon Y:=\left(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2g+1}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2g+1}_{-1}\right)^{\mathrm{ss}}\big/\mathbb{C}^{*}=\mathbb{C}^{2}\times\mathrm{Tot}_{\mathbb{P}^{2g}}\left(\mathcal{O}(-1)^{2g+1}\right)\to X(d).

Here,
small
means that
\dim Y\times_{X(d)}Y=\dim X(d)
and
Y\times_{X(d)}Y
has a unique irreducible component of maximal dimension.
Then, by the BBDG decomposition theorem, we have that
\tau_{*}\mathrm{IC}_{Y}=\mathrm{IC}_{X(d)}
.
We decorate the BPS sheaves with a superscript zero to indicate that the potential is zero.
We obtain that:

(11.1)

\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d}=\tau_{*}\mathrm{IC}_{Y}=\mathrm{IC}_{X(d)}\text{ and }\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{(1,0)}=\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{(0,1)}=\mathrm{IC}_{\mathbb{C}}.

Proposition 11.3
.

(i) If
v
is odd, then
we have an equivalence
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}D^{b}(Y)
and
\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d,v}=\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d}
.

(ii) If
v
is even, then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}=\langle D^{b}(Y),D^{b}(\mathbb{C}^{2})\rangle,\ \mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d,v}=\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d}\oplus\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{(1,0)}\boxtimes\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{(0,1)}.

Proof.

The category
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))
is generated by the line bundles
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}(w\beta_{2}+(v-w)\beta_{1})
for
w\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

(11.2)

\frac{v}{2}\leqslant w\leqslant 2g+1+\frac{v}{2}.

One can show that
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}
is equivalent to the “window subcategory” (in the sense of
[
HL15
]
) of
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{\prime}(d))
containing objects
F
such that the weights of
\mathbb{C}^{*}
on
F|_{0}
are contained in
\left[v/2,v/2+2g+1\right]\cap\mathbb{Z}
.

If
v
is odd, then
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}\cong D^{b}(Y)
by
[
HL15
, Theorem 2.10]
. The boundary points

v/2
and
v/2+2g+1
are not integers, so
\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d,v}=\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d}
.

If
v
is even, then
\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d,v}=\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d}\oplus\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{(1,0)}\boxtimes\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{(0,1)}
.
The fixed locus of the unique Kempf-Ness locus in the construction of
Y
is
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2g+1}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2g+1}_{-1})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}=\mathbb{C}^{2}
.
As a corollary of
[
HL15
, Theorem 2.10]
, see the remark in
[
HLS16
, Equation (3)]
, the category
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}
has a semiorthogonal decomposition with summands
D^{b}(Y)
and
D^{b}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
.
∎

As a corollary of the above proposition and of the computations (
11.1
), we obtain the following equality:

\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v})\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\dim H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d,v})=\begin{cases}2g+1,\text{ if }v\text{ is odd},\\
2g+2,\text{ if }v\text{ is even}.\end{cases}

The equality
(*)
is also the consequence (
8.17
) of Theorem
8.14
.
Note that the dimensions of
K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v})
can be computed immediately using Proposition
11.1
and (
11.2
), and then
(*)
can be checked without using window categories.
However, by Proposition
11.3
, the equality
(*)
is obtained as a corollary of the Atiyah-Hirzebruch theorem for the smooth varieties
Y
and
\mathbb{C}^{2}
.

Further, Proposition
11.3
is useful when considering a non-zero potential for
Q
.
For example, consider the potential

W:=\sum_{i=1}^{2g+1}e_{i}\overline{e}_{i}.

Note that
\mathop{\rm Tr}W\colon Y\to\mathbb{C}
is smooth. The computation (
11.1
) implies that:

(11.3)

\mathcal{BPS}_{d}=\tau_{*}\varphi_{\mathop{\rm Tr}W}\mathrm{IC}_{Y}[-1]=0,\ \mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{(1,0)}=\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{(0,1)}=\mathrm{IC}_{\mathbb{C}}.

In this case, the monodromy actions of
BPS sheaves are trivial.
Further, Proposition
11.3
implies that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(d)_{v}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{MF}(Y,\mathop{\rm Tr}W)=0\text{ if }v\text{ is odd},

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(d)_{v}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{MF}(Y,\mathop{\rm Tr}W),\mathrm{MF}(\mathbb{C}^{2},0)\rangle\simeq\mathrm{MF}(\mathbb{C}^{2},0)\text{ if }v\text{ is even}.

Let
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The following equality (which also follows by (
8.17
)) holds by a direct computation:

\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}=\dim H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,v})=\begin{cases}0,\text{ if }v\text{ is odd},\\
1,\text{ if }v\text{ is even}.\end{cases}

Remark 11.4
.

A similar analysis can be done for any symmetric quiver
Q=(I,E)
and a dimension vector
d=(d^{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
such that
d^{i}\in\{0,1\}
for every
i\in I
. We do not give the details for the proofs. Let
v\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\gcd(\underline{d},v)=1
. Assume
W=0
.

One can show that, for a generic GIT stability
\ell\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}\cong M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}
, the GIT quotient
Y:=R(d)^{\ell\text{-ss}}/G(d)

gives a small resolution

\tau\colon Y\to X(d)
.
Then
\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d}=\tau_{*}\mathrm{IC}_{Y}.

By
[
HLS20
]
, there is an equivalence:

\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}D^{b}(Y)
.
The following equality (which is a corollary of Theorem
8.14
) follows then by the Atiyah-Hirzebruch theorem for the smooth variety
Y
:

\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}=\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}=\dim H^{\ast}(Y)=\dim H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{0}_{d}).

Similar computations can be done also for a general
v\in\mathbb{Z}
.

11.2.
Quivers with one vertex and an odd number of loops

For
g\in\mathbb{N}
, let

Q
be the quiver with one vertex and
2g+1
loops. The following is a picture for
g=1
.

(11.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}

For
d\in\mathbb{N}
, recall the good moduli space map:

\mathscr{X}(d):=\mathfrak{gl}(d)^{\oplus(2g+1)}/GL(d)\to X(d):=\mathfrak{gl}(d)^{\oplus(2g+1)}/\!\!/GL(d).

For
g>0
, the variety
X(d)
is singular.
For every stability condition
\ell\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}
, we have that
\mathscr{X}(d)^{\ell\text{-ss}}=\mathscr{X}(d)
, so we do not obtain resolutions of singularities of
X(d)
as in the previous example.
There are no known crepant geometric resolutions (in particular, small resolutions) of
X(d)
. For
\gcd(d,v)=1
, Špenko–Van den Bergh
[
ŠVdB17
]
