proved that
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}
is a twisted noncommutative crepant resolution of
X(d)
. In view of Theorem
8.18
, we regard
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}
as a categorical analogue of a small resolution of
X(d)
.

Reineke
[
Rei12
]
and Meinhardt–Reineke
[
MR19
]
provided multiple combinatorial formulas for the dimensions of the intersection cohomologies
\mathrm{IH}^{i}(X(d))
.
As noted in Remark
11.2
,
the result of Theorem
8.18
also provides combinatorial formulas for the total intersection cohomology of
X(d)
. We explain that our formula recovers a formula already appearing in the work of Reineke
[
Rei12
, Theorem 7.1]
.

Fix
v\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By Proposition
11.1
, we need to determine the number of (integral, dominant) weights
\chi=\sum_{i=1}^{d}c_{i}\beta_{i}\in M(d)^{+}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{d}c_{i}=v
and
c_{i}\geqslant c_{i-1}
for every
2\leqslant i\leqslant d
, such that

(11.5)

\chi+\rho-v\tau_{d}\in\frac{2g+1}{2}\mathrm{sum}[0,\beta_{i}-\beta_{j}],

where the Minkowski sum is after all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant d
.
We define
\widetilde{\chi}\in M(d)
and
\widetilde{c}_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant d
as follows:

\widetilde{\chi}:=\chi-g\cdot(2\rho)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\widetilde{c}_{i}\beta_{i}.

Note that, for every
2\leqslant i\leqslant d
, the inequality
c_{i}\geqslant c_{i-1}
becomes:

(11.6)

\widetilde{c}_{i}-\widetilde{c}_{i-1}+2g\geqslant 0.

A dominant weight
\chi
satisfies (
11.5
) if and only if, for all dominant cocharacters
\lambda
of
T(d)\subset GL(d)
, we have:

(11.7)

\langle\lambda,\chi+\rho-v\tau_{d}\rangle\leqslant\frac{2g+1}{2}\langle\lambda,\mathfrak{g}^{\lambda>0}\rangle=(2g+1)\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle.

Proposition 11.5
.

The inequalities (
11.7
) hold for all dominant cocharacters
\lambda
if and only they hold for the cocharacters
\lambda_{k}(z)=(\overbrace{1\ldots,1}^{d-k},\overbrace{z,\ldots,z}^{k})\in T(d)
for
1\leqslant k\leqslant d
.

Proof.

In the cocharacter lattice, any dominant cocharacter
\lambda
is a linear combination with nonnegative coefficients of
\lambda_{k}
for
1\leqslant k\leqslant d
. Then, if (
11.7
) holds for all
\lambda_{k}
, it also holds for all dominant
\lambda
.
∎

The conditions (
11.7
) for
\lambda_{k}

is equivalent to
that
\langle\lambda_{k},\widetilde{\chi}\rangle\leqslant\langle\lambda_{k},v\tau_{d}\rangle
, or
equivalently

(11.8)

\sum_{i=d-k+1}^{d}\widetilde{c}_{i}\leqslant\frac{vk}{d}.

Definition 11.6
.

Let
\mathscr{H}^{2g+1}_{d,v}
be the set of tuplets of integers
(\widetilde{c}_{i})_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
satisfying the inequality (
11.6
) and (
11.8
) for every
2\leqslant k\leqslant d
and such that
\sum_{i=1}^{d}\widetilde{c}_{i}=v
.
Let
H^{2g+1}_{d,v}:=\#\mathscr{H}^{2g+1}_{d,v}
.

Remark 11.7
.

The numbers
H^{2g+1}_{d,0}
appear in combinatorics as “score sequences of complete tournaments”, and in the study of certain
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-fixed points in the moduli of
SL(n)
-Higgs bundles, see
[
Rei12
, Section 7]
. By Proposition
11.1
, we have that:

\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}=H^{2g+1}_{d,v}.

By Theorem
8.18
, for any
v\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\gcd(d,v)=1
, we obtain that:

(11.9)

\dim\mathrm{IH}^{\ast}(X(d))=H^{2g+1}_{d,v}.

The above statement was already proved (by different methods) by Reineke and Meinhardt–Reineke by combining
[
Rei12
, Theorem 7.1]
and
[
MR19
, Theorem 4.6]
, see also
[
MR19
, Section 4.3]
. Note that we assume that the number of loops is odd in order to apply Theorem
8.14
. In loc. cit., Reineke also provided combinatorial formulas for
m
-loop quivers for
m
even.

Remark 11.8
.

Note that, as a corollary of (
11.9
), we obtain that
H^{2g+1}_{d,v}=H^{2g+1}_{d,v^{\prime}}
if
\gcd(d,v)=\gcd(d,v^{\prime})=1
. There are natural bijections
\mathscr{H}^{2g+1}_{d,v}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{H}^{2g+1}_{d,v^{\prime}}
for
d|v-v^{\prime}
or for
v^{\prime}=-v
, but we do not know such natural bijections for general
v,v^{\prime}
coprime with
d
.

For
\gcd(d,v)=1
and
n\geqslant 1
, the topological
K-theory

K_{i}^{\rm{top}}(\mathbb{M}(nd)_{nv})

is computed from the intersection cohomology of
X(e)
for
e\in\mathbb{N}
, and the set
S_{nv}^{nd}
.
The following is a corollary of Proposition
8.5
:

Corollary 11.9
.

For
\gcd(d,v)=1
and
n\geqslant 1
,
the set
S_{nv}^{nd}
consists of partitions
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
of

nd
such that
d_{i}=n_{i}d

for
(n_{i})_{i=1}^{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
a partition of
n
.

Example 11.10
.

Suppose that
g=0
. In this case, the
variety
X(d)
is smooth:

X(d)=\mathfrak{gl}(d)/\!\!/GL(d)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathrm{Sym}^{d}(\mathbb{C})\cong\mathbb{C}^{d}.

The above isomorphism is given by sending an element of
\mathfrak{gl}(d)
to the
set of generalized eigenvalues.
However
X(d)^{\mathrm{st}}=\emptyset
if
d>1
,
thus
\mathcal{BPS}_{d}=\mathrm{IC}_{\mathbb{C}}
if

d=1
, and
\mathcal{BPS}_{d}=0
for
d>1
.
Then by Corollary
11.9
, we have

\mathcal{BPS}_{d,v}=0
unless
d|v
, case in which
\mathcal{BPS}_{d,v}=\mathrm{Sym}^{d}(\mathcal{BPS}_{1})=\mathrm{IC}_{X(d)}
.
Thus for
g=0
, we have

(11.10)

\displaystyle\dim H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}_{d,v})=\begin{cases}1,\text{ if }d|v,\\
0,\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

On the other hand, by
[
Tod23b
, Lemma 3.2]
we have that
\mathbb{M}(d)_{v}=0
unless
d|v
, case in which it is the subcategory of
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))
generated by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}(d)}(v\tau_{d})
, and thus equivalent to
D^{b}(X(d))
, see
[
Tod23b
, Lemma 3.3]
. Then:

(11.11)

\displaystyle\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}=\begin{cases}1,\text{ if }d|v,\\
0,\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

For
g=0
, we can thus verify (
8.17
) by the direct computations (
11.10
), (
11.11
).

11.3.
The three loop quiver

In this subsection, we make explicit the corollary of Theorem
8.14
for the three loop quiver
Q
as in (
11.4
) with loops
\{x,y,z\}
and with potential
W=x[y,z]
.
Note that
(Q,W)
is the tripled quiver
with potential of the quiver
Q^{\circ}
with one
vertex and one loop.
The quasi-BPS categories
\mathbb{S}(d)_{v}

in this case are studied in
[
PTa
]

as a categorification of BPS invariants
for
\mathbb{C}^{3}
,
and they are admissible
subcategories
of the DT category
\mathcal{DT}(d)

for
\mathbb{C}^{3}
studied in
[
PTa
]
. The quasi-BPS categories
\mathbb{T}(d)_{v}

for the double of
Q^{\circ}
are admissible
subcategories
in
D^{b}(\mathscr{C}(d))
, where
\mathscr{C}(d)
is the
derived moduli stack of zero dimensional
sheaves on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
with length
d
.
In
[
PTa
, Theorem 4.13]
, we
computed torus-equivariant algebraic K-theory of

\mathbb{T}(d)_{v}
.
The following is an analogue of
[
PTa
, Theorem 4.13]

for topological K-theory:

Proposition 11.11
.

Let
(d,v)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Z}
be coprime, let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then we have the following identities:

\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}(nd)_{nv})_{\mathbb{Q}}=\dim K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbb{T}(nd)_{nv})_{\mathbb{Q}}=p_{2}(n),

where
p_{2}(n)
is the number of partitions of
n
.

Proof.

It is proved in
[
Davb
, Theorem 5.1]

that

\mathcal{BPS}_{e}

is isomorphic to
\Delta_{\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathbb{C}^{3}}
for every
e\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\Delta\colon\mathbb{C}^{3}\hookrightarrow X(e)
is the subvariety parameterizing three diagonal matrices. Then
\dim H^{\ast}(X(e),\mathcal{BPS}_{e})=1
, and so

\mathrm{Sym}^{k}\left(H^{\ast}(X(e),\mathcal{BPS}_{e})\right)

is one dimensional
for every positive integers
e
and
k
.
Then
H^{\ast}(X(nd),\mathcal{BPS}_{A})
is also one dimensional for every
A\in S^{nd}_{nv}
. Note that
\#S^{nd}_{nv}=p_{2}(n)
by Corollary
11.9
.
Then

H^{\ast}(X(nd),\mathcal{BPS}_{nd,nv})=\bigoplus_{A\in S^{nv}_{nd}}H^{\ast}(X(nd),\mathcal{BPS}_{A})=\mathbb{Q}^{\oplus p_{2}(n)}.

The monodromy is trivial on
H^{\ast}(X(nd),\mathcal{BPS}_{nd,nv})
. By Theorems
8.14
and
10.6
, we obtain
the desired computations.
∎

Remark 11.12
.

By Theorem
8.14
, the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories may be determined whenever one can compute the BPS cohomology and the set
S^{v}_{d}
. Proposition
11.11
is an example of such a computation.
We mention two other computations for the three loop quiver with potentials
W^{\prime}:=x[y,z]+z^{a}
(for
a\geqslant 2
) and
W^{\prime\prime}:=x[y,z]+yz^{2}
.

Let
\mathcal{BPS}^{\prime}_{d}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(d)_{v}
be the BPS sheaves and the quasi-BPS categories of
(Q,W^{\prime})
. Denote similarly the BPS sheaves and the quasi-BPS categories of
(Q,W^{\prime\prime})
.
By
[
DP22
, Theorem 1.5]
, we have that
H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{\prime}_{d})^{\mathrm{inv}}=0
because
H^{\ast}(\mathbb{C},\varphi_{t^{a}})^{\mathrm{inv}}=0
. Then Theorem
8.14
implies that, for every
i,v\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\gcd(d,v)=1

we have
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}=0
.

By
[
DP22
, Corollary 7.2]
, we have that
H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{\prime\prime}_{d})^{\mathrm{inv}}=H^{\ast}(X(d),\mathcal{BPS}^{\prime\prime}_{d})
is one dimensional. As in Proposition
11.11
, we have that, for every
i,v\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the dimension of
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime\prime}(d)_{v})_{\mathbb{Q}}

equals
p_{2}(\gcd(d,v))
.

12.
Étale covers of preprojective algebras

In this section, we prove an extension of Theorem
10.6
to étale covers of preprojective stacks which we use to compute the topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories of K3 surfaces in
[
PTd
]
.

We will use the notations and constructions from Subsection
10.1
.
Throughout this section, we fix a quiver
Q^{\circ}=(I,E^{\circ})
and a dimension vector
d\in\mathbb{N}^{I}
.
We begin by discussing the setting and by stating Theorem
12.2
, the main result of this section.

12.1.
Preliminaries

Let
E
be an affine variety with an action of
G:=G(d)
and with a
G
-equivariant étale map

e\colon E\to R^{\circ}(d)\oplus R^{\circ}(d)^{\vee}.

We assume that the induced map

\displaystyle e\colon U:=E/\!\!/G\to Y(d)

is étale. In this subsection, we use the notation

(-)_{U}
by the pull-back for the above map. So from the diagram

\textstyle{\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{cl}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{P}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{Y}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{P(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y(d)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces X(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}}

the pull-back with respect to
e\colon U\to Y(d)
is denoted by

\textstyle{\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{cl}}_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{P}(d)^{\rm{red}}_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{P}(d)_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{Y}(d)_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}(d)_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{U}}
\scriptstyle{f_{U}}
\textstyle{P(d)_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y(d)_{U}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces X(d)_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{U}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}.}

Definition 12.1
.

Let
\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d}\in\mathrm{Perv}(P(d))
be the preprojective BPS sheaf.
We define

(12.1)

\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,U}:=e^{*}(\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d})\in\mathrm{Perv}(L).

Here
e\colon P(d)_{U}\to P(d)
is the pull-back from
e\colon U\to Y(d)
.
We also define
\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,\delta,U}
for
\delta\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}
as in (
10.2
).

We take
\delta\in M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}^{W_{d}}
such that
\langle 1_{d},\delta\rangle=v\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By Theorem
3.5
and Remark
3.6
, there is a semiorthogonal decomposition:

D^{b}\left(\mathscr{P}(d)\right)_{v}=\big\langle\mathbb{A}(d;\delta),\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)\big\rangle.

The purpose of this section is to prove the following:

Theorem 12.2
.

Let
\langle 1_{d},\delta\rangle=v\in\mathbb{Z}
.
There exist subcategories
\mathbb{T}_{U}=\mathbb{T}(L;\delta)_{U}
and
\mathbb{A}_{U}=\mathbb{A}(L;\delta)_{U}
of
D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)_{U})_{v}
such that:

(1)

there is a semiorthogonal decomposition
D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)_{U})_{v}=\langle\mathbb{A}_{U},\mathbb{T}_{U}\rangle
,

(2)

if
U=P(d)
and
e
is the identity, then
\mathbb{T}_{U}=\mathbb{T}(d;\delta)
and
\mathbb{A}_{U}=\mathbb{A}(d;\delta)
,

(3)

if
h\colon E^{\prime}\to E
is a
G
-equivariant étale map inducing
an étale map

\displaystyle e^{\prime}:=e\circ h\colon U^{\prime}:=E^{\prime}/\!\!/G\to Y(d)

and if we consider the categories
\mathbb{A}_{U^{\prime}}
,

\mathbb{T}_{U^{\prime}}
for
E^{\prime}
,
then
h
induces functors

\displaystyle h^{*}\colon\mathbb{T}_{U}\to\mathbb{T}_{U^{\prime}},\ h^{*}\colon\mathbb{A}_{U}\to\mathbb{A}_{U^{\prime}},

(4)

for any
i,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, the cycle map (
5.11
) for
\mathscr{L}
induces isomorphisms

\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{T}_{U})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{-2\ell-i}(P(d)_{U},\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,\delta,U}).

Further, one can also define categories
\mathbb{T}^{\mathrm{red}}_{U},\mathbb{A}^{\mathrm{red}}_{U}\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)_{U})_{v}
which satisfy the analogous conditions to (1)-(4) above. In particular, the map
l^{\prime}\colon\mathscr{P}(d)_{U}^{\mathrm{red}}\to\mathscr{P}(d)_{U}
induces an isomorphism

(12.2)

\mathrm{c}\circ l^{\prime}_{*}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{T}_{U}^{\mathrm{red}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{T}_{U})_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{-2\ell-i}(L,\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,\delta,U}).

We will only explain the constructions for
\mathscr{P}(d)
, the case of
\mathscr{P}(d)_{U}^{\mathrm{red}}
is similar.
In Subsection
12.2
, we define the categories
\mathbb{T}
and
\mathbb{A}
using graded matrix factorizations and the Koszul equivalence. In Subsection
12.3
, we prove the third claim in Theorem
12.2
.

12.2.
Quasi-BPS categories for étale covers

We will use the setting from Subsection
12.1
.
There is a Cartesian diagram, where the horizontal maps
e
are étale maps:

(12.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(d)_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(d)_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(d).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X,d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

By Theorem
3.5
, there is a semiorthogonal decomposition

(12.4)

D^{b}\big(\mathscr{X}(d)\big)_{v}=\big\langle\mathbb{B}(d;\delta),\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)\big\rangle.

We define subcategories
\mathbb{B}_{U}=\mathbb{B}(d;\delta)_{U}
,
\mathbb{M}_{U}=\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)_{U}
of
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))_{v}
to be
classically generated (see Subsection
13.4
) by
e^{\ast}\mathbb{B}(d;\delta)
,
e^{\ast}\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)
respectively.
Then Lemma
13.3
implies that:

Corollary 12.3
.

There is a semiorthogonal decomposition

(12.5)

\displaystyle D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d)_{U})_{v}=\langle\mathbb{B}_{U},\mathbb{M}_{U}\rangle.

Consider the category of graded matrix factorizations
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X}(d)_{U},f_{U})
, where the grading is given by weight two
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-action on
fibers of
\mathscr{X}(d)_{U}\to\mathscr{Y}(d)_{U}
.
By the Koszul equivalence, we have that:

(12.6)

\kappa_{L}\colon D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)_{U})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathscr{X}(d)_{U},f_{U}).

Define the subcategories of
D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)_{U})
:

\mathbb{T}_{U}:=\kappa_{L}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathbb{M}_{U},f_{U})\right),\ \mathbb{A}_{U}:=\kappa_{L}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathbb{B}_{U},f_{U})\right).

By
[
PTa
, Proposition 2.5]
, we obtain:

Corollary 12.4
.

The properties (1), (2), and (3) in the statement of Theorem
12.2
hold for the categories
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{T}
of
D^{b}(\mathscr{P}(d)_{U})
.

12.3.
Comparison with BPS cohomology

Recall the notation from Subsection
10.1
.
Recall the BPS sheaf
\mathcal{BPS}_{d}\in\mathrm{Perv}(X(d))
for the tripled quiver with potential
(Q,W)
associated to
Q^{\circ}
.
We define the following perverse sheaf,
see the diagram (
12.3
):

\mathcal{BPS}_{d,U}=e^{*}(\mathcal{BPS}_{d})\in\mathrm{Perv}(X(d)_{U}).

For
\delta\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}
, we define

\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta,U}\in D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X(d)))

to be the direct sum
as in (
8.7
).
On the other hand, the monodromy
on
H^{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}(d)_{U},\varphi_{f_{U}})
is trivial, so
we have the cycle map

(12.7)

\displaystyle\mathrm{c}\colon

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{a}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}(d)_{U},f_{U})\right)_{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\to H^{2\dim\mathscr{F}-i-2a}(\mathscr{X}(d)_{U},\varphi_{f_{U}}\mathbb{Q}[-1])\oplus H^{2\dim\mathscr{F}-i-2a}(\mathscr{X}(d)_{U},\varphi_{f_{U}}\mathbb{Q}[-2]).

For
\delta\in M(d)_{\mathbb{R}}^{W_{d}}
, we set

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{U}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)_{U},f_{U}),\ \mathbb{S}_{U}^{\rm{gr}}:=\mathrm{MF}^{\rm{gr}}(\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)_{U},f_{U}).

We now define a cycle map from topological K-theory of quasi-BPS categories to BPS cohomology, which is the analogue of Theorem
8.14
.

Proposition 12.5
.

For
\delta\in M(d)^{W_{d}}_{\mathbb{R}}
, the
cycle map (
12.7
) induces the morphism

(12.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{a}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathbb{S}_{U}\right)\to

\displaystyle H^{\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-i-2a}(X(d)_{U},\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta,U})

\displaystyle\oplus H^{\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-i-2a}(X(d)_{U},\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta,U}[-1]).

Proof.

The same argument used in the proof of Theorem
8.15
applies here. The
\lambda
-widths (see (
9.17
)) of the category
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)_{U}
are equal to the
\lambda
-widths of the category
\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)
for all cocharacters
\lambda
.
The analogue of Proposition
9.8
then holds for

\mathbb{S}_{U}
.
∎

We next prove the analogue of Theorem
8.14
.

Proposition 12.6
.

The cycle map (
12.8
) is an isomorphism over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

Similarly to the proof of Theorem
8.14
, it is enough to show that both
sides in (
12.8
) have the same dimension.
By pulling back the isomorphism in Theorem
8.26
, and noting
that the monodromy for
f_{U}
is trivial, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X(d)_{U}}(\mathbb{S}_{U})_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta,U}[\beta^{\pm 1}]\oplus\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta,U}[\beta^{\pm 1}][1].

Therefore, the dimensions of both sides in (
12.8
) are equal.
∎

We prove the analogue of Theorem
10.6
.

Proposition 12.7
.

The cycle map induces an isomorphism

(12.9)

\mathrm{c}\colon\mathrm{gr}_{a}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\left(\mathbb{S}_{U}^{\rm{gr}}\right)_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\dim\mathscr{X}(d)-i-2a}(X(d)_{U},\mathcal{BPS}_{d,\delta,U}).

Further, there is an isomorphism:

\mathrm{gr}_{a}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{T}_{U})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{-2a-i}(P(d)_{U},\mathcal{BPS}^{p}_{d,\delta,U})

Proof.

The isomorphism (
12.9
) follows from Proposition
7.6

and Proposition
12.6
. The last isomorphism follows from (
12.9
) and the compatibility between dimensional reduction and the Koszul equivalence from Proposition
7.6
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
12.2
.

The first three properties hold by Corollary
12.4
. The fourth property follows from Proposition
12.7
.
The statement for reduced stacks follows similarly. The isomorphism (
12.2
) also follows directly from Proposition
5.10
.
∎

We also note the following analogue of Corollary
10.2
.

Proposition 12.8
.

The following Chern character map
is injective over
\mathbb{Q}

\mathrm{ch}\colon K^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathbb{T}_{U})\hookrightarrow G^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{P}(d)_{U})\to\widetilde{H}_{i}^{\rm{BM}}(\mathscr{P}(d)_{U}).

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of Corollary
10.2
.
The claim follows from Proposition
6.11
, a framed version of
the semiorthogonal decomposition (
12.5
)

\displaystyle D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\rm{ss}}_{U})=\langle\mathbb{B}_{U}^{\prime},\mathbb{M}_{U}^{\prime}\rangle

and the Koszul equivalence.
∎

13.
Some auxiliary results

13.1.
Proof of Proposition
3.7

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)=\mathcal{S}_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup\mathcal{S}_{N}\sqcup\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\rm{ss}}

be the Kempf-Ness stratification with center
\mathcal{Z}_{i}\subset\mathcal{S}_{i}

and associated cocharacters
\lambda_{i}\colon\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\to T(d)
,
see
[
HL15
, Section 2]
for the above notions.
Let
\mathbb{G}^{\alpha}(d;\delta)

be the subcategory of
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d))
consisting
of objects
\mathcal{E}
such that
\mathcal{E}|_{\mathcal{Z}_{i}}
has

\lambda_{i}
-weights contained in the interval

\displaystyle\left[-\frac{1}{2}n_{\lambda_{i}}^{\alpha},\frac{1}{2}n_{\lambda_{i}}^{\alpha}\right]+\langle\lambda_{i},\delta\rangle,

where
n_{\lambda}^{\alpha}
is defined by

\displaystyle n_{\lambda}^{\alpha}:=\langle\lambda,(R^{\alpha f}(d)^{\vee})^{\lambda>0}-(\mathfrak{g}(d)^{\vee})^{\lambda>0}\rangle,

where
R^{\alpha f}(d)=R(d)\oplus V^{\alpha}(d)
as in (
3.2
).
By the window theorem
[
HL15
,
BFK19
]
, the following
composition is an equivalence

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{\alpha}(d;\delta)\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d))\to D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\rm{ss}}).

By
[
Tod24
, Lemma 5.1.9]
, the cocharacter
\lambda_{i}
acts on

V^{\alpha}(d)
with strictly negative weights.
Therefore
n_{\lambda}^{\alpha}>n_{\lambda_{i}}
, so the pull-back
along
\pi_{\alpha f}\colon\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)\to\mathscr{X}(d)

restricts to the functor

\displaystyle\pi_{\alpha f}^{\ast}\colon\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)\to\mathbb{G}^{a}(d;\delta).

The above functor admits a right adjoint given by

\displaystyle\Phi\colon\mathbb{G}^{a}(d;\delta)\xrightarrow{p_{v}\circ\pi_{\alpha f\ast}}D^{b}(\mathscr{X}(d))_{v}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\mathbb{M}(d;\delta).

The first functor is the composition of

\pi_{\alpha f\ast}
with the projection
onto the weight
v
-part, and
i^{R}
is the
projection with respect to the semiorthogonal decomposition (
3.17
).
Then we have
\Phi\circ\pi_{\alpha f}^{\ast}=\operatorname{id}
, thus the functor

\displaystyle\pi_{\alpha f}^{\ast}\colon\mathbb{M}(d;\delta)\to D^{b}(\mathscr{X}^{\alpha f}(d)^{\rm{ss}})

is fully-faithful with right adjoint.
∎

13.2.
Action of exterior algebra on the K-theory of matrix factorizations

In this subsection, we prove Proposition
7.5
.
We denote by
\mathrm{pt}:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}
.
The following computation follows as in Lemma
5.12
.

Lemma 13.1
.

As a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-algebra, we have

\displaystyle K_{\ast}^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{pt},0))=\Lambda:=\mathbb{Z}[\epsilon]

where
\epsilon
has degree one.

Proof.

Let
T:=\operatorname{Spec}\left(\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}\right)
.
Since
\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{pt},0)
is equivalent to
D^{b}(T)/\mathrm{Perf}(T)

by Theorem
2.1
,
there are exact sequences

\displaystyle\cdots\to K_{i}(T)\to G_{i}(T)\to K_{i}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{pt},0))\to K_{i+1}(T)\to\cdots.

As
K_{i}(T)=G_{i}(T)=\mathbb{Z}
, it is enough to show that

K_{i}(T)\to G_{i}(T)
is zero, which follows as in Lemma
5.12
.
∎

Note that, for any regular function on a smooth stack
h\colon\mathscr{Y}\to\mathbb{C}
, the category
\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{Y},h)
is a module over
\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{pt},0)
,
so
K_{\ast}^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{Y},h))

is a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded

\Lambda
-module by Lemma
13.1
.

Proposition 13.2
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a smooth stack. Then

K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\ast}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X},0))\cong K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\ast}(\mathscr{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda

as
\Lambda
-modules.
Moreover, if
\mathbb{M}\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X})
is an admissible subcategory of
D^{b}(\mathscr{X})
, there is an isomorphism of
\Lambda
-modules:

K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\ast}(\mathrm{MF}(\mathbb{M},0))\cong K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\ast}(\mathbb{M})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the first isomorphism.
Let
\mathscr{X}_{0}
be the derived zero locus of
0\colon\mathscr{X}\to\mathbb{C}
.
By the long exact sequence (
6.14
), it suffices to show that the map
\alpha^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(\mathscr{X}_{0})\to D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{0})
induces the zero map:

\alpha^{\prime}\colon K^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}_{0})\to G^{\mathrm{top}}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{X}_{0}),

which we showed in Lemma
5.12
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.5
.

It is enough to prove (
7.18
).
Recall the notation
\mathrm{pt}:=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}
and
T:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}
and the Koszul equivalence (
2.11
). Using
[
HLP20
, Proposition 3.24]

(also see
[
Tod23a
, Proposition 3.9]
and note that

\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{pt},0)\simeq D^{b}(T)/\mathrm{Perf}(T)
), the equivalence (
2.11
) induces an equivalence:

\kappa^{\prime}\colon D^{b}(\mathscr{P})\otimes_{D^{b}(\mathrm{pt})}\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{pt},0)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{E}^{\vee},f).

Let
\mathscr{P}_{0}:=\mathscr{P}\times T
and let
\pi\colon\mathscr{P}_{0}\to\mathscr{P}
and
t\colon T\to\mathrm{pt}
be the natural projections. We have that
\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{pt},0)\cong D^{b}(T)/t^{*}(D^{b}(T))
. Then
there is an equivalence

D^{b}(\mathscr{P})\otimes_{D^{b}(\mathrm{pt})}\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{pt},0)\simeq D^{b}(\mathscr{P}_{0})/\pi^{*}(D^{b}(\mathscr{P})).

It suffices to show that the map

\pi^{*}\colon G^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{P})\to G^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{P}_{0})\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}G^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}(\mathscr{P})

is zero, which follows as in the proof of Lemma
5.12
.
∎

13.3.
Proof of Lemma
4.3

Proof.

We use the construction of
\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}
via stable motivic homotopy theory,
see
[
Mou19
, Proposition 7.7]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{M}^{\rm{top}}(\mathscr{D})=\mathrm{Betti}_{M}(KGL_{M}(\mathscr{D})).

Here
\mathrm{Betti}_{M}
is the Betti realization of the stable
motivic category

\displaystyle\mathrm{Betti}_{M}\colon\mathrm{SH}(M)\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(M^{\rm{an}})

and
KGL_{M}(\mathscr{D})\in\mathrm{SH}(M)
is the constant spectrum
associated to the homotopy K-theory

\underline{KH}_{M}(\mathscr{D}):=L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\underline{K}_{M}(\mathscr{D}))
,
see
[
Mou19
, Section 7.3]
for details of the notation.

We have the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{SH}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Betti}_{U}}
\scriptstyle{j_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(U^{\rm{an}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{SH}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Betti}_{M}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(M^{\rm{an}}).}

The above diagram may not be commutative as
j
is not proper.
However, there is a natural morphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{Betti}_{M}\circ j_{\ast}\to j_{\ast}\circ\mathrm{Betti}_{U}

which is an equivalence when applied to
\Lambda
-constructible objects
for a fixed class of objects
\Lambda\subset\mathrm{SH}(\mathrm{pt})
, where
\mathrm{pt}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}
,
see
[
Ayo10
, Corollary 3.17]
.
In particular, we apply it for the object

\displaystyle KGL_{U}(\mathrm{Perf}(U))=\phi^{\ast}KGL_{\mathrm{pt}}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathrm{pt}))

for the structure morphism
\phi\colon U\to\mathrm{pt}
to obtain the equivalence

\displaystyle\mathrm{Betti}_{M}\circ j_{\ast}KGL_{U}(\mathrm{Perf}(U))\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}j_{\ast}K_{U}^{\rm{top}}(\mathrm{Perf}(U)).

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle j_{\ast}KGL_{U}(\mathrm{Perf}(U))\simeq KGL_{M}(\mathrm{Perf}(U)).

The above equivalence follows from the construction of
KGL_{M}
and noting
that, for a smooth morphism
Y\to M
, we have

\displaystyle\underline{K}_{M}(\mathrm{Perf}(U))(Y)

\displaystyle=K(\mathrm{Perf}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{M}}\mathrm{Perf}(Y))

\displaystyle=K(\mathrm{Perf}(Y_{U})

\displaystyle=\underline{K}_{U}(\mathrm{Perf}(U))(Y_{U})

where
Y_{U}=Y\times_{M}U
. Therefore we obtain the desired equivalence (
4.9
).
∎

13.4.
Base-change and semiorthogonal decompositions

In this paper, we need to construct semiorthogonal decompositions for étale maps (or open immersions) of moduli of representations of a quiver. We use the following base-change result for semiorthogonal decompositions, see
[
Kuz11
]
for the case of derived categories of varieties.

For a pretriangulated dg-category
\mathscr{D}
and a subcategory
\mathscr{C}\subset\mathscr{D}
, we say
that
\mathscr{D}

is classically generated by

\mathscr{C}
if the smallest pretriangulated subcategory of
\mathscr{D}

which contains
\mathscr{C}
and is closed under direct summands is
\mathscr{D}
.

Proposition 13.3
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a QCA (quasi-compact with affine stabilizers) derived stack with a morphism

\pi\colon\mathscr{X}\to S
to a scheme
S
. Let

D^{b}(\mathscr{X})=\langle\mathscr{C}_{i}\mid i\in I\rangle

be a
S
-linear semiorthogonal decomposition.
Then, for any étale map
f\colon T\to S
and
f_{T}\colon\mathscr{X}_{T}=\mathscr{X}\times_{S}T\to\mathscr{X}
,
there is a semiorthogonal decomposition

D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})=\langle\mathscr{C}_{i,T}\mid i\in I\rangle,

where
\mathscr{C}_{i,T}\subset D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})
is the subcategory classically
generated by
f_{T}^{\ast}\mathscr{C}_{i}
.

Proof.

The image of
f_{T}^{\ast}\colon\operatorname{Ind}D^{b}(\mathscr{X})\to\operatorname{Ind}D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})

classically generates
\operatorname{Ind}D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})
, as
any
A\in\operatorname{Ind}D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})
is a direct summand of
f_{T}^{\ast}f_{T\ast}A
. Indeed, consider the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{X}_{T}\times_{\mathscr{X}}\mathscr{X}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T}}

Then
f_{T}^{*}f_{T*}A=g_{T*}g_{T}^{*}A=A\otimes g_{T*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}
. The map
g_{T}
has a section given by the diagonal map
\Delta\colon\mathscr{X}_{T}\to\mathscr{X}^{\prime}
, thus
g_{T*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}
has
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}_{T}}
as a direct summand, and so
A
is indeed a direct summand of
f_{T}^{\ast}f_{T\ast}A
.

By the QCA assumption, objects in
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})\subset\operatorname{Ind}D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})
are compact,
see
[
DG13
]
.
Therefore
D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})
is classically generated by

f_{T}^{\ast}D^{b}(\mathscr{X})
, thus by
\mathscr{C}_{i,T}
for
i\in I
.

To show semiorthogonality,
consider
i,j\in I
such that
\operatorname{Hom}(A_{i},A_{j})=0
for all
A_{i}\in\mathscr{C}_{i}
and
A_{j}\in\mathscr{C}_{j}
.
We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{D^{b}(\mathscr{X}_{T})}(f_{T}^{\ast}A_{i},f_{T}^{\ast}A_{j})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Ind}D^{b}(\mathscr{X})}(A_{i},f_{T\ast}f_{T}^{\ast}A_{j})

(13.1)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Ind}D^{b}(\mathscr{X})}(A_{i},A_{j}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{T}).

Here
f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{S}\in D_{\rm{qc}}(S)=\operatorname{Ind}\mathrm{Perf}(S)
,
and the
S
-linearity of
\mathscr{C}_{j}
implies

A_{j}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{T}\in\operatorname{Ind}\mathscr{C}_{j}
.
Then the vanishing of (
13.1
) follows from
the compactness of
A_{i}
(see the end of the proof of
[
PTd
, Lemma 5.5]
for how compactness is used).
∎
